<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every finitely generated group quasi-isometric to a finitely generated virtually polycyclic group is virtually polycyclic. This resolves the lattice-recognition conjecture of Eskin, Fisher and Whyte, which allows the ambient solvable Lie group in the conclusion to differ from the one in the hypothesis.

- **claim-virtual-polycyclic-recognition** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-recognition)): Let $P$ be a finitely generated virtually polycyclic group, and let $H$ be any finitely generated group. If $H$ and $P$ are quasi-isometric, then $H$ is virtually polycyclic.
  - Explanation: Virtual polycyclicity is preserved by quasi-isometry among finitely generated groups, without an initial solvability assumption on the comparison group.
- **claim-solvable-lattice-recognition** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-lattice-recognition)): If a finitely generated group $H$ is quasi-isometric to a lattice in a connected simply connected solvable Lie group, then a finite-index subgroup of $H$ is a uniform lattice in some connected simply connected solvable Lie group.
  - Explanation: The comparison group has a finite-index uniform lattice realization, with permission for the ambient solvable Lie group to change.
- **claim-uniform-bounded-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-bounded-height)): Let $G$ be a connected simply connected real-triangulable unimodular Lie group, with a fixed left invariant Riemannian metric, exponential height map $\pi:G\to W$, and a fixed norm on $W$. There is a finite subgroup $\mathcal A_G\leq\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(W)$ such that, for every $K\geq1$ and $C\geq0$, there is a constant $B=B(G,K,C)$ with the following property. Every $(K,C)$ self quasi-isometry $F$ of $G$ has an $A_F\in\mathcal A_G$ satisfying $$\label{eq:height-main} \bigl\\|\pi F(g)-\pi F(1)-A_F\pi(g)\bigr\\|\leq B \qquad(g\in G).$$
  - Explanation: Every self quasi-isometry preserves canonical height up to a member of a fixed finite linear group and a bounded error controlled by the quasi-isometry constants.
- **claim-cartan-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cartan-model)): There is a real generalized weight decomposition $$\label{eq:cartan-weight-decomposition} \mathfrak g=\bigoplus_\gamma\mathfrak g_\gamma, \qquad \mathfrak g_0=\mathfrak d, \qquad [\mathfrak g_\gamma,\mathfrak g_\eta] \subseteq\mathfrak g_{\gamma+\eta}.$$ Let $\mathfrak n$ be the subalgebra generated by the spaces with nonzero weight. Then $\mathfrak n$ is a nilpotent ideal contained in $[\mathfrak g,\mathfrak g]$, and $\mathfrak g=\mathfrak n+\mathfrak d$. There are homogeneous generators $X_1,\ldots,X_\nu$ of $\mathfrak n$ whose images form a basis of $\mathfrak n/[\mathfrak n,\mathfrak n]$ and whose weights $\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_\nu$ are all nonzero.* *The groups $N=\exp_G(\mathfrak n)$ and $D=\exp_G(\mathfrak d)$ are closed and simply connected, and $N$ is normal in $G$. With the conjugation action of $D$ on $N$, multiplication defines a surjective Lie homomorphism and submersion $$\label{eq:cartan-multiplication} q:N\rtimes D\longrightarrow G,\qquad q(n,d)=nd.$$ Its kernel is $$\label{eq:cartan-overlap-kernel} \ker q=\{(a^{-1},a):a\in N\cap D\}.$$ Moreover, $N\cap D=\exp_G(\mathfrak n\cap\mathfrak d)$. Every compact subset of $G$ has lifts in a compact subset of $N\rtimes D$. Finally, $N=1$ if and only if $G$ is nilpotent.
  - Explanation: Generalized Cartan weights produce a nilpotent normal factor and a nilpotent Cartan factor whose multiplication surjects onto the ambient group, retaining their overlap.
- **claim-nilpotent-scaling** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-d-scaling)): There is a constant $C\ge1$, depending only on $D$, the metric, and the splitting, such that for $d=\exp_D(\sum_jv_j)$, $$\begin{aligned} \\|v_j\\|&\le C(1+\|d\|_D)^j \quad(1\le j\le s), \label{eq:d-coordinate-upper}\\ \|d\|_D&\le C\left(1+\max_{1\le j\le s}\\|v_j\\|^{1/j}\right). \label{eq:d-coordinate-reverse} \end{aligned}$$ The group laws transported from $D$ by $\Delta_r$ converge, with all derivatives on compact coordinate sets, to the law of $D_\infty$ as $r\to\infty$. Every compact $K\subset D_\infty$ satisfies $\sup_{z\in K}\|\Delta_r z\|_D=O_K(r)$ for $r\ge1$. Conversely, for every fixed $C_0$, all $\Delta_r^{-1}\{d:\|d\|_D\le C_0r\}$, $r\ge1$, lie in one compact coordinate set. If $z_r,z'_r$ belong to a fixed compact coordinate set and their coordinate difference tends to zero, then $$\label{eq:d-scaling-equicontinuity} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}_D(\Delta_r z_r,\Delta_r z'_r)=o(r).$$ For $v\in V_1$ one also has $$\label{eq:d-horizontal-flow-limit} \Delta_r^{-1}\bigl(\Delta_r z\exp_D(rt v)\bigr) \longrightarrow z\exp_\infty(tv),$$ uniformly when $z,t,v$ range over fixed compact sets. Haar measures on $D$ and $D_\infty$ are Lebesgue measure in these exponential coordinates, up to fixed scalars. If both scalars are chosen equal and $Q=\sum_jj\dim V_j$, then $\Delta_r$ multiplies Haar volume by $r^Q$, and $$\label{eq:d-polynomial-volume} c t^Q\le \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_D\{d:\|d\|_D\le t\}\le C t^Q \qquad(t\ge1)$$ for some $c>0$. In particular $D$ has doubling Haar measure: $$\label{eq:nilpotent-doubling} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,2t)\le C\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,t)\qquad(t>0).$$ Every conclusion of this lemma holds for an arbitrary connected, simply connected nilpotent Lie group $L$ equipped with a left invariant Riemannian metric and a splitting of its lower central filtration, with $L$ in place of $D$ and constants allowed to depend on $L$.
  - Explanation: Intrinsic nilpotent coordinates have polynomial length bounds, converge under scaling to the graded group, and give polynomial Haar volume and doubling.
- **claim-canonical-cartan-height** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-canonical-height)): Each generator weight $\alpha_i$ vanishes on $[\mathfrak d,\mathfrak d]+(\mathfrak d\cap\mathfrak n)$, and inclusion of $\mathfrak d$ into $\mathfrak g$ induces an isomorphism $$\label{eq:canonical-cartan-height} W\cong V\Big/\bigcap_{i=1}^\nu\ker\alpha_i, \qquad V=\mathfrak d/[\mathfrak d,\mathfrak d].$$ Under this identification, $\pi$ kills $N$ and its restriction to $D$ is the displayed quotient of its abelianization. When $\nu=0$, the intersection on the right is all of $V$, and $W=0$; this occurs exactly when $G$ is nilpotent.* *Write $\mathfrak n_\gamma=\mathfrak n\cap\mathfrak g_\gamma$ and view every weight occurring in $\mathfrak n$ as a linear form on $W$. For fixed norms, there are constants $C,M$ such that, for every $d\in D$ and every such weight $\gamma$, $$\label{eq:d-polynomial-unipotent} \mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}_d\|_{\mathfrak n_\gamma} =e^{\gamma(\pi d)}U_\gamma(d),\qquad \\|U_\gamma(d)\\|+\\|U_\gamma(d)^{-1}\\| \le C(1+\|d\|_D)^M,$$ where $U_\gamma(d)$ is unipotent. For $w\in W$, the linear map $$\label{eq:exact-weight-dilation} S_w\|_{\mathfrak n_\gamma}=e^{\gamma(w)}\mathop{\mathrm{id}}$$ is a Lie algebra automorphism and hence a group automorphism of $N$. Both $S_w$ and conjugation by $d$ preserve Haar measure on $N$. Consequently product Haar measure $dn\,dd$ is Haar measure on $N\rtimes D$.* *The height map on $D_\infty$ is the homomorphism induced by $V_1\cong V\to W$, and it satisfies $$\label{eq:d-height-scaling} \pi(\Delta_r z)=r\pi(z) \qquad(z\in D_\infty, r>0).$$
  - Explanation: The canonical height quotient is recovered from generator weights on the Cartan abelianization. Conjugation separates exponential scalar factors from polynomial unipotent factors, and the relevant actions preserve Haar measure.
- **claim-weighted-box-calculus** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-box-calculus)): There are constants depending only on the fixed Lie model with the following properties, uniformly for $r\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$.* 1. *The unit cube in a maximizing scaled basis and the Minkowski sum of the intervals $[-e^{rh_Z(E)}Z,e^{rh_Z(E)}Z]$, $Z\in\mathcal Z$, contain constant multiples of one another. Different maximizing bases therefore give boxes comparable by fixed changes of $L$.* 2. *In these coordinates all Lie bracket coefficients are bounded uniformly. Multiplication and inversion in exponential coordinates are polynomials of uniformly bounded degree and coefficients. For some $C,b$, a product of $k\ge1$ elements of $B_r(E,L)$ lies in $$B_r(E,C(1+k)^b(1+L)^b).$$* 3. *Writing $d_N=\dim N$, Haar measure satisfies $$\label{eq:box-volume} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_N B_r(E,L)\asymp L^{d_N}e^{rP(E)}.$$* 4. *Exact weight dilations shift the template: $S_{rw}B_r(E,L)$ is comparable, with fixed radius factors, to $B_r(E+w,L)$. For each fixed $C_0$ there are $C,b$ such that, for $r\ge1$ and $\|d\|_D\le C_0r$, $$\label{eq:box-conjugation} \mathop{\mathrm{Ad}}_d B_r(E,L)\subseteq B_r\left(E+\frac{\pi d}{r},C(1+r)^b(1+L)^b\right).$$ The same assertion applies to $d^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: Maximal-width coordinates give uniformly comparable boxes, controlled nilpotent products, their Haar volume, and polynomial losses under Cartan conjugation.
- **claim-finite-support-symmetries** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-support-symmetries)): The function $P$ is translation invariant, depends only on the convex hull, and is Lipschitz for Hausdorff distance. The function $$p(w)=P([0,w])$$ is a polyhedral norm on $W$. The group $$\label{eq:support-symmetry-group} \mathcal A=\{A\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(W):P(AE)=P(E)\text{ for every nonempty compact convex }E\subset W\}$$ is finite. Its pullback action permutes the oriented rays of the generator weights $\alpha_i$.
  - Explanation: The support functional defines a polyhedral segment norm. Its full template-preserving linear group is finite and permutes oriented generator-weight rays.
- **claim-uniform-generator-schemes** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-product-schemes)): For each fixed $L<\infty$ there are integers and constants depending only on $L$ and the Lie model such that every element of $B_r(E,L)$, for every $r\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$, is the endpoint of a scheme of at most that many factors, with all $\|t_j\|$ bounded by that constant. Choices may be made Borel measurably in the endpoint.* *For any sequence of these normalized coordinate systems there is a subsequence and one fixed generator product scheme whose normalized polynomial maps converge, with bounded degree and coefficients, to a map submersive at an interior point of a fixed parameter cube. The parameter point may be chosen to map to the identity.* *If $r\to\infty$ and the radius is $e^{o(r)}$, generation still uses a bounded number of factors, now with amplitudes $e^{rs_i(E)+o(r)}$. Finally, every element of $N$ is a product of at most a fixed number of the elements $\exp(tX_i)$, with unrestricted real parameters.
  - Explanation: Weighted boxes admit bounded generator product representations and measurable parameter choices. Subsequence limits retain a fixed scheme with a submersive endpoint map.
- **claim-logarithmic-distortion** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-log-distortion)): There is a constant $C$ such that $$\|n\|_G\le C\log(2+\\|\log_Nn\\|)\qquad(n\in N).$$ For fixed constants controlling $\|d\|_D/r$ and the amplitude factor, if $\|d\|_D=O(r)$, $\|t\|\le e^{rs_i(E)+o(r)}$, and $\alpha_i(\pi d)=rs_i(E)+o(r)$, then the distance between $xnd$ and $xn\exp(tX_i)d$ is $o(r)$, uniformly in $x,n$. With bounded amplitude factor and exact equality of heights, this distance is $O(\log(2+r))$.
  - Explanation: Nilpotent displacements have logarithmic ambient length, and generator switches at their supporting heights have sublinear cost, with logarithmic cost in the exact-height regime.
- **claim-support-visiting-paths** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-support-switch-paths)): Fix a compact convex $E_V\subset V$, a point $v_0\in E_V$, a constant $L$, and pivots $d_0\in D$ satisfying $a_D(d_0)=rv_0$ and $\|d_0\|_D\le C_0r$, where $r\ge1$. Every $u\in B_r(\pi E_V,L)$ can be reached from $d_0$ at endpoint $ud_0$ by a path with the following properties:* 1. *it uses a bounded number of $D$-legs and generator switches, has length at most $Cr$, and all its $D$-positions have intrinsic length at most $Cr$;* 2. *on each $D$-leg the abelianized position lies in $rE_V$; each connecting switch has length at most $C\log(2+r)$;* 3. *for any fixed continuous homomorphism $l:G\to W$, all path values of $l$ lie within $O_l(\log(2+r))$ of $r\,l(E_V)$, where $l$ on $V$ denotes its induced linear map.* *Choices may be made measurably in $u$. The analogous statements with switch length $o(r)$ hold for a subexponential box radius. In particular, for a fixed compact convex $E\subset W$ and any pivot of intrinsic length at most $C_0r$ with $\pi d_0\in rE$, the same construction reaches $ud_0$, $u\in B_r(E,L)$, with absolute heights in $rE+O(\log(2+r))$ and total length $O(r)$. The constants in this specialization are independent of the pivot’s other abelianized coordinates. One may also use a prescribed $D$-curve of length $O(r)$ and intrinsic positions $O(r)$ whose height image attains all the required supports, by visiting and reversing portions of that curve.
  - Explanation: Generator schemes can be implemented by paths visiting supporting Cartan heights and returning to a pivot, with controlled length and height excursions.
- **claim-controlled-height-path-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-path-packing)): Fix $C_0>0$ and a separation distance $a>0$. There are constants $C,b$ such that the following holds for $r\ge1$. Let $E\subset W$ be compact and contain zero. The endpoints of paths starting at one point $x$, of length at most $C_0r$, and satisfying $$\pi(x^{-1}\gamma(t))\in rE \quad\hbox{at every point of each path}$$ have $a$-separated subsets of cardinality at most $$\label{eq:finite-path-packing} C(1+r)^b e^{rP(E)}.$$ Each endpoint has a relative lift $(u,d)$ with $$u\in B_r(E,C(1+r)^b),\qquad \|d\|_D\le Cr.$$ These containments remain valid, after increasing $C,b$, under fixed bounded right thickening in $N\rtimes D$.* *In particular, along a sequence with relative path heights in $r(E+o(1))$ and length $O(r)$, the upper separated count is $e^{rP(E)+o(r)}$, and the lifts have $u\in B_r(E,e^{o(r)})$, $\|d\|_D=O(r)$. Without a height restriction, a path of length $t$ has a relative lift with $\|d\|_D\le C(1+t)$ and $\\|\log_Nu\\|\le e^{C(1+t)}$.
  - Explanation: Path endpoints with controlled length and height have exponentially bounded separated counts with polynomial losses. The argument uses lifts without requiring injective multiplication coordinates.
- **claim-polynomial-bounded-height-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial-path-packing)): Fix $M,C_0,a>0$. There are $C,b$ such that, for every $t\ge0$, the endpoints of paths starting at one point $x$, of length at most $C_0t$, whose every relative height has norm at most $M$, have $a$-separated subsets of cardinality at most $C(1+t)^b$. The constants depend only on the displayed data and the fixed Lie model. This lemma also holds when $N=1$.
  - Explanation: Uniformly bounded height throughout each path yields a genuinely polynomial endpoint packing bound, including the nilpotent case.
- **claim-fixed-position-packing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-fixed-sheet-packing)): For every $a>0$ there is $C_a<\infty$ such that, for every $d\in D$, $x\in G$, and bounded measurable $S\subset N$, the set $\{xnd:n\in S\}$ contains an $a$-separated subset with cardinality at least $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_N(S)/C_a$. The constant is independent of $x,d,S$. For a fixed finite number of such slices the corresponding assertion holds for product Haar measure and the product maximum metric.
  - Explanation: At a fixed Cartan position, actual nilpotent Haar volume supplies a separated-point lower bound uniformly in the position and basepoint.
- **claim-variable-lift-multiplicity** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-lift-multiplicity)): Fix a radius $R$. There are $C,b,c$ such that, for $T,H\ge0$ and any metric ball $B_G(y,R)$, the set $$\{u\in N:\text{ for some }d\in D, \|d\|_D\le T, \ \\|\pi d\\|\le H,\ q(u,d)\in B_G(y,R)\}$$ has $N$-Haar outer measure at most $C(1+T)^b e^{cH}$. In particular, if $T=O(r)$ and $H=o(r)$, this measure is $e^{o(r)}$, with one common envelope for any family subject to those bounds.
  - Explanation: A projected metric ball hides at most a controlled nilpotent coordinate volume when Cartan length and height are bounded, including contributions from the overlap.
- **claim-polynomial-image-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-polynomial-image)): Fix integers $a\ge b\ge1$ and $D\ge1$. There are constants $C>0$ and $A_1,A_2>0$, depending only on $a,b,D$, with the following property. Let $\Phi:\mathbb R^a\to\mathbb R^b$ be a polynomial map of degree at most $D$, all of whose coefficients have absolute value at most $B\ge0$. Suppose that one $b$-column minor of $D\Phi$ has a coefficient of absolute value at least $c>0$. Put $c_0=\min\{c,1\}$. For every $R\ge1$ and every Lebesgue-measurable $E\subset[-R,R]^a$ of measure $m>0$, $$\label{eq:polynomial-image-quantitative} \|\Phi(E)\|_{\mathrm{in}} \ge C c_0^{b+1}(1+B)^{-b^2} m^{A_1}(1+R)^{-A_2}.$$ One may take, with $d=\max\{1,b(D-1)\}$, $$A_1=1+ad(b+1),\qquad A_2=a+a^2d(b+1)+Db^2.$$* *In particular, let $r\to\infty$ along a sequence and let $\Phi_r$ have these fixed dimensions and degree bound. Suppose their coefficients converge to those of a polynomial map which is submersive at some point. If the $E_r$ are Lebesgue-measurable and $$E_r\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a,\qquad 1\le R_r\le e^{o(r)},\qquad \|E_r\|\ge e^{-o(r)},$$ then $$\label{eq:polynomial-image-subexponential} \|\Phi_r(E_r)\|_{\mathrm{in}}\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ The same conclusion follows from <a href="#eq:polynomial-image-quantitative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:polynomial-image-quantitative">[eq:polynomial-image-quantitative]</a> if the coefficient upper bounds $B_r$ and a chosen minor-coefficient lower bound $c_r$ satisfy $\log(1+B_r)+\log(1/\min\{c_r,1\})=o(r)$.
  - Explanation: A nonzero derivative-minor coefficient gives a quantitative inner-volume lower bound for polynomial images of measurable sets. Submersive coefficient limits preserve subexponential image volume.
- **claim-backward-recurrence** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-backward-recurrence)): Let $J$ be a second countable unimodular locally compact group with Haar measure $\mu$. Let $Q\subset J$ be compact with $0<\mu(Q)<\infty$, and let $S\subset Q$ be Borel with $\mu(S)>0$. Fix an integer $h\ge1$ and compactly supported probability measures $\sigma_1,\ldots,\sigma_h$ on $J$. Suppose that numbers $D_j\ge1$ satisfy $$\mu(Q\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma_j)\le D_j\mu(Q) \qquad(1\le j\le h).$$ There are nested Borel sets $$S_0\subset S_1\subset\cdots\subset S_h=S$$ with positive measure such that, writing $a_j=\mu(S_j)/\mu(Q)$ and $\tau_j=a_j/(2D_j)$, one has $$\label{eq:recurrence-mass} a_{j-1}\ge\frac{a_j^2}{2D_j}$$ and $$\label{eq:recurrence-transition} \mathbb P\{x u_j\in S_j\}\ge\tau_j \qquad(x\in S_{j-1}),$$ where $u_j$ has law $\sigma_j*\check\sigma_j$. If the $u_j$ are sampled independently, every $x\in S_0$ therefore has probability at least $\prod_{j=1}^h\tau_j$ of satisfying $$x u_1\cdots u_j\in S_j\subset S \qquad(1\le j\le h).$$ In a sequence indexed by $r\to\infty$, if $h$ is fixed, $\mu(S)/\mu(Q)\ge e^{-o(r)}$, and every $D_j\le e^{o(r)}$, then $\mu(S_0)/\mu(Q)$ and the displayed success probability are at least $e^{-o(r)}$.
  - Explanation: Backward selection retains nested positive-measure starting sets and lower transition probabilities, allowing a fixed sequence of independent switches to remain in a prescribed Borel set.
- **claim-restricted-product-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-restricted-products)): Let $r\to\infty$ along a sequence. Fix $h\ge1$, positive integers $a_1,\ldots,a_h$, and $a=\sum_j a_j$. For each $r$, let $J_r,Q_r,S_r,\mu_r$, and $\sigma_{j,r}$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:backward-recurrence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:backward-recurrence">18</a>, with $$\frac{\mu_r(S_r)}{\mu_r(Q_r)}\ge e^{-o(r)},\qquad \frac{\mu_r(Q_r\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\sigma_{j,r})}{\mu_r(Q_r)} \le e^{o(r)}.$$ For each $j$, let $\theta_j\in\mathbb R^{a_j}$ have a probability law $\nu_{j,r}$, and let the $\theta_j$ be independent. Suppose their joint law $\nu_r$ gives full measure to a Borel admissible domain $\Theta_r\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a$, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure, and satisfies $$1\le R_r\le e^{o(r)},\qquad \frac{d\nu_r}{d\theta}\le e^{o(r)} \quad\text{almost everywhere}.$$ Let $v_{j,r}:\mathbb R^{a_j}\to J_r$ be Borel maps whose images of the laws $\nu_{j,r}$ are $\sigma_{j,r}*\check\sigma_{j,r}$. Finally, let $\Phi_r:\mathbb R^a\to\mathbb R^b$, $1\le b\le a$, be polynomial maps of a fixed degree bound whose coefficients converge to a map submersive somewhere.* *There is a starting point $x_r\in S_r$ such that the successful parameter set $$E_r=\left\{\theta\in\Theta_r: x_r v_{1,r}(\theta_1)\cdots v_{j,r}(\theta_j)\in S_r \text{ for every }1\le j\le h\right\}$$ satisfies $$\|E_r\|\ge e^{-o(r)},\qquad \|\Phi_r(E_r)\|_{\mathrm{in}}\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ Here $\Phi_r$ may record a product endpoint, several successive endpoints, or a tuple of bundle products; it need not record the intermediate states used in the definition of $E_r$.
  - Explanation: Independent increment sampling, a density upper bound, and a fixed submersive polynomial limit retain both successful parameter measure and endpoint inner volume after intermediate-state restrictions.
- **claim-lipschitz-representatives** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-lipschitz-pairs)): For each $K\ge1$ and $C\ge0$, there are constants $B<\infty$ and $\Lambda\ge1$, depending only on $G,K,C$, with the following property. Every $(K,C)$ self quasi-isometry of $G$ is within distance $B$ of a map $F$ belonging to a pair $(F,\bar F)$ such that both maps are $\Lambda$-Lipschitz, both satisfy $$d(J(g),J(h))\ge \Lambda^{-1}d(g,h)-\Lambda \qquad(J=F,\bar F),$$ and $$d(\bar F(F(g)),g)\le\Lambda,\qquad d(F(\bar F(g)),g)\le\Lambda \qquad(g\in G).$$
  - Explanation: Coarse quasi-isometries and their coarse inverses can be replaced by nearby uniformly Lipschitz pairs while retaining lower metric bounds and bounded composition errors.
- **claim-compact-pair-sections** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pair-sections)): The spaces $X$ and $Y$ are compact metrizable. There are homeomorphisms $$G\times X\longrightarrow\Omega,\quad (z,\xi)\longmapsto L_z\xi, \qquad Y\times G\longrightarrow\Omega,\quad (\eta,x)\longmapsto\eta\cdot x.$$ The formulas $$T_x\xi=L_{F_\xi(x)^{-1}}(\xi\cdot x),\qquad U_y\eta=(L_{y^{-1}}\eta)\cdot\bar F_\eta(y)$$ define continuous right actions on $X$ and $Y$, respectively. In particular, $T_yT_x=T_{xy}$ and $U_zU_y=U_{yz}$.
  - Explanation: Normalized forward and inverse sections are compact metrizable, provide product coordinates on the pair space, and carry continuous normalized right actions.
- **claim-ergodic-cohomological-slope** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-cohomological-slope)): Let $\mu$ be an invariant ergodic probability on $X$. There is a continuous homomorphism $l:G\to W$ and functions $\phi_j\in L^2(X,\mu;W)$ such that, for every compact $Q\subset G$, $$\label{eq:compact-coboundaries} \sup_{x\in Q} \bigl\\|b(x)+\phi_j\circ T_x-\phi_j-l(x)\bigr\\|_{L^2(\mu)} \longrightarrow0.$$ The homomorphism $l$ kills $N$ and factors through $V$ on $D$. For each fixed $0<R<\infty$, $$\label{eq:stationary-sublinear} \lim_{r\to\infty}\frac1r\sup_{\|x\|_G\le Rr} \\|\pi F(x)-l(x)\\|_{L^2(\mu)}=0.$$ These assertions concern the fixed measure $\mu$; no rate uniform over invariant measures is asserted.
  - Explanation: Reduced-cohomology vanishing gives a height homomorphism and compact coboundary approximations for a fixed ergodic invariant probability, yielding averaged sublinear height error.
- **claim-averaged-slope-volume** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-stationary-volume)): Let $\sigma$ be a probability on $X$, and let $l:G\to W$ be a continuous homomorphism killing $N$. Suppose <a href="#eq:stationary-sublinear" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stationary-sublinear">[eq:stationary-sublinear]</a> holds with $\sigma$ in place of $\mu$. Regard $l\|_D$ as a linear map on $V$. Then every nonempty compact convex $E_V\subset V$ satisfies $$\label{eq:forward-slope-volume} P(\pi E_V)\le P(lE_V).$$ Consequently $l=A\pi$ for an invertible linear map $A:W\to W$. The same assertion holds for the inverse maps on $Y$.
  - Explanation: Accessible source volume and image path packing force an averaged slope to detect exactly the canonical height quotient through an invertible linear map.
- **claim-matched-inverse-slope** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-inverse-coupling)): Let $\mu$ be an invariant probability on $X$ and let $A\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(W)$. Suppose there are $\phi_j\in L^2(X,\mu;W)$ satisfying <a href="#eq:compact-coboundaries" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:compact-coboundaries">[eq:compact-coboundaries]</a> with $l=A\pi$. There is a finite nonzero $U$-invariant measure $\nu$ on $Y$ and functions $\theta_j\in L^2(Y,\nu;W)$ such that, for every compact $Q\subset G$, $$\label{eq:inverse-coboundaries} \sup_{y\in Q} \bigl\\|\pi\bar F(y)+\theta_j\circ U_y-\theta_j-A^{-1}\pi y \bigr\\|_{L^2(\nu)}\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular, for every fixed $0<R<\infty$, $$\label{eq:inverse-stationary-sublinear} \lim_{r\to\infty}\frac1r\sup_{\|y\|_G\le Rr} \\|\pi\bar F(y)-A^{-1}\pi y\\|_{L^2(\nu)}=0.$$
  - Explanation: Product Haar coordinates on the pair space transfer the given forward slope to precisely its inverse, using whole-fiber averages rather than traces on a null section.
- **claim-finite-stationary-slopes** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-stationary-slopes)): For every invariant ergodic probability $\mu$ on $X$, there is an $A_\mu\in\mathcal A$ such that <a href="#eq:compact-coboundaries" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:compact-coboundaries">[eq:compact-coboundaries]</a> holds with $l=A_\mu\pi$ and $$\lim_{r\to\infty}\frac1r\sup_{\|x\|_G\le Rr} \\|\pi F(x)-A_\mu\pi x\\|_{L^2(\mu)}=0 \qquad(0<R<\infty).$$ The coupled finite invariant measure on $Y$ satisfies the analogous estimate with $A_\mu^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: Forward volume comparison and the coupled inverse comparison force every ergodic stationary slope into the finite support-symmetry group.
- **claim-stationary-increments** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cor-stationary-increments)): Fix an invariant probability $\mu$ on $X$, without assuming ergodicity. For each $v\in V$, represented in the fixed degree-one complement in $\mathfrak d$, one has $$\label{eq:finite-stationary-increments} \int_X\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}\left( \frac{\pi F_\xi(\exp_D(Lv))}{L}, \{A\pi v:A\in\mathcal A\}\right)\,d\mu(\xi) \longrightarrow0\qquad(L\to\infty).$$ For each fixed $L>0$, the integrand is a bounded continuous function on $X$. For $L\ge1$ these functions have a common bound depending only on $v,G,\Lambda$. The convergence is for the fixed $\mu$.
  - Explanation: Ergodic decomposition extends the stationary conclusion to a fixed nonergodic invariant probability by taking distance to the full finite set of possible increments.
- **claim-profile-interior-slack** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-interior-slack)): Use the data of Definition <a href="#def:chart-profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:chart-profiles">28</a>. Suppose a family $u_r\in N$ has a word expansion of its logarithm whose coefficient of each word $Z$ is bounded in absolute value by $$\exp\bigl(r(1-c\delta)h_Z(\mathcal C)+o(r)\bigr),$$ with a common error envelope for the finitely many words. Then $k_0u_rk_0^{-1}$ has logarithmic coordinates tending exponentially to zero in the coordinates normalized for $B_r(\mathcal C)$. The same conclusion holds uniformly for bounded families satisfying the displayed coefficient bounds with a common envelope.
  - Explanation: The strict interior height margin dominates conjugation by an enlarged suffix, making normalized logarithmic coordinates decay exponentially.
- **claim-invariant-occupation-limits** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-occupation-invariance)): Let $\rho$ be a continuous compactly supported probability density with respect to Haar measure on $D_\infty$, with support contained in $\mathcal U_\delta$. Independently of $a$, sample $z$ with density $\rho$. Every weak limit of the laws of $$\omega[xak_0\Delta_r z]\in X$$ is an invariant probability for the normalized right action of $G$.
  - Explanation: Sampling Cartan positions strictly inside the allowed height region yields occupation laws whose weak limits are invariant under the normalized group action.
- **claim-profile-convex-increments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-convex-increments)): Let $\nu$ be a weak limit of chart-profile laws, and let $f\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}\nu$. If $$J=\{z\exp_\infty(tv):0\le t\le T\}\Subset\mathcal U_\delta, \qquad T>0,\quad v\in V,$$ then $$\label{eq:profile-convex-increments} \frac{f(z\exp_\infty(Tv))-f(z)}{T} \in\operatorname{conv}\{A\pi v:A\in\mathcal A\}.$$
  - Explanation: Horizontal secants of a supported limiting profile lie in the convex hull of stationary height increments.
- **claim-profile-segment-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-profile-segment-volume)): Under the hypotheses of Lemma <a href="#lem:profile-convex-increments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:profile-convex-increments">31</a>, $$\label{eq:profile-segment-volume} P(f(J))\ge P(\pi J)=T p(\pi v).$$
  - Explanation: Restricted recurrent switches create sufficient endpoint volume to prevent the support exponent of a horizontal segment from decreasing under a limiting profile.
- **claim-deterministic-profile-affinity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-affinity-criterion)): Let $f:\mathcal U_\delta\to W$ be continuous and locally Lipschitz along horizontal segments, with locally uniform bounds for unit horizontal velocities. Suppose that, for every segment $J=\{z\exp_\infty(tv):0\le t\le T\}\Subset\mathcal U_\delta$, $T>0$, both $$\label{eq:affinity-two-inequalities} \frac{f(z\exp_\infty(Tv))-f(z)}{T} \in\operatorname{conv}(\mathcal A\pi v), \qquad P(f(J))\ge T p(\pi v)$$ hold. Then there exist $A\in\mathcal A$ and $b\in W$ such that $f(z)=A\pi z+b$ for all $z\in\mathcal U_\delta$.
  - Explanation: Convex increment constraints together with segment volume prevent folding. Horizontal derivatives become finite-valued labels, and hypoelliptic regularity makes the label constant.
- **claim-affine-profile-limits** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-affine-profiles)): For every fixed $\delta$ with $0<c\delta<1/2$ and every sequence in Definition <a href="#def:chart-profiles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:chart-profiles">28</a>, the profile laws are relatively compact. Every function in the support of every limiting law has the form $$\label{eq:affine-profile-limit} f(z)=A\pi z+b\quad(z\in\mathcal U_\delta), \qquad A\in\mathcal A,\quad b\in W.$$
  - Explanation: Every supported chart-profile limit is affine on the interior chart domain, although its label and offset may initially depend on the profile.
- **claim-affine-class-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-affine-profile-concentration)): Fix $\delta$ as above, a function $\varepsilon(r)\ge0$ tending to zero, a nonempty compact set $Z\subset\mathcal U_\delta$, and $\eta>0$. Uniformly over the fixed family of map pairs, all $x\in G$, and all deterministic $k_0\in B_r((1+\delta)\mathcal C,e^{r\varepsilon(r)})$, $$\label{eq:distance-to-affine-class} \mathbb P_a\left\{ \min_{A\in\mathcal A}\inf_{b\in W} \sup_{z\in Z}\\|f_{r,x,ak_0}(z)-A\pi z-b\\|\ge\eta \right\}\longrightarrow0.$$ The conclusion also holds when $k_0$ is sampled independently of $a$ from any probability supported in the same allowed suffix set. In that case every supported limiting profile is still affine on $\mathcal U_\delta$.
  - Explanation: Finite-scale profiles concentrate uniformly near the affine class on compact chart subsets for a prescribed vanishing suffix envelope. This does not yet select a common affine map.
- **claim-neighboring-linear-labels** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-neighbor-labels)): There is $\delta_1>0$, depending only on the fixed group and the uniform quasi-isometry bounds, with the following property. Fix $0<\delta\leq\delta_1$. Let $F$, $x$, and the following suffixes and steps vary with $r$: $$\begin{gathered} a\sim\frac{1_{B_r(\mathcal C)}}{\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}B_r(\mathcal C)}\,dn, \qquad n=ak_0,\qquad k_0\in B_r((1+\delta)\mathcal C,e^{o(r)}),\\ k=\exp(tX_i), \quad \|t\|\leq e^{(1+\delta)r s_i(\mathcal C)+o(r)}. \end{gathered}$$ The suffix and step are deterministic. Every pair in the support of a limiting joint law of $(f_{r,x,n},f_{r,x,nk})$ has the form $$(A\pi z+b_1,\ A\pi z+b_2)\qquad(z\in\mathcal U_\delta)$$ for a common $A\in\mathcal A$ and some $b_1,b_2\in W$.
  - Explanation: Neighboring sheets share a linear label. Different labels would produce a facet reflection whose alternating target products have too much terminal volume.
- **claim-endpoint-tuple-lift-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-tuple-lift-volume)): Fix a positive integer $h$ and compact sets $E_1,\ldots,E_h\subset W$ containing $0$. Suppose that, for each $r$, there are $M_r$ tuples $(y_1,\ldots,y_h)\in G^h$, separated by a sufficiently large fixed constant in the maximum product metric. Let $y_0$ be a common basepoint. Suppose each tuple admits lifts relative to $y_0$ of the form $$y_0^{-1}y_j=q(U_1\cdots U_j,d_j),\qquad U_j\in B_r(E_j,e^{o(r)}),\qquad \|d_j\|_D\leq Cr,\quad \\|\pi d_j\\|=o(r),$$ with common bounds for all tuples. Then there is a measurable set $\mathcal T_r\subset N^h$ of increments satisfying the same box bounds, with possibly larger subexponential factors, such that $$\label{eq:tuple-lift-volume} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_{N^h}(\mathcal T_r)\geq M_r e^{-o(r)}.$$ Every product $u_1\cdots u_h$ from $\mathcal T_r$ has an accompanying $d\in D$ with $\|d\|_D=O(r)$ and $\pi d=o(r)$ whose projection lies a bounded distance from one of the listed terminal points $y_0^{-1}y_h$. Moreover, the $N$-coordinate volume of all such pairs projecting into any one ball of fixed radius in $G$ is at most $e^{o(r)}$.
  - Explanation: Separated endpoint tuples yield a large measurable set of nilpotent increments. Variable Cartan lifts have subexponential overlap over fixed projected balls.
- **claim-cone-quotients** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cone-quotients)): Let $S\subset W^*$ be either the closed positive ray generated by a generator weight $\alpha$, or the closed cone generated by two linearly independent generator weights $\alpha,\lambda$. The span $\mathfrak i_S$ of the mixed words is a $\mathfrak d$-invariant ideal of $\mathfrak n$ containing $\mathfrak d\cap\mathfrak n$. Put $$I_S=\exp_N(\mathfrak i_S),\qquad L_S=N/I_S.$$ The group $I_S$ is closed, and $L_S$ is connected, simply connected, and nilpotent. Every compact subset of $L_S$ has compact lifts in $N$ and hence in $G$. There is a well-defined action of $G$ on $L_S$ under which $N$ acts by left translations and $D$ acts by its induced conjugation automorphisms. Write $$p_S(g)=g\cdot 1_{L_S}.$$ Then $p_S(gh)=g\cdot p_S(h)$ and the stabilizer of $1_{L_S}$ is $I_SD$. There is a linear form $\ell_S\in W^*$ such that, for every Haar-measurable $E\subset L_S$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_{L_S}(g\cdot E) =e^{\ell_S(\pi g)}\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_{L_S}(E). \label{eq:cone-haar-multiplier}$$ The form $\ell_S$ is the sum of the weights of $\mathfrak n/\mathfrak i_S$, counted with their dimensions. It can be expressed as a nonnegative integral combination of the inside generator weights, with a positive coefficient for every inside generator. In particular, it is a positive multiple of $\alpha$ in the ray case, and it has the form $c\alpha+d\lambda$, with $c,d>0$, in the two-generator case.
  - Explanation: Mixed words define invariant ideals containing the Cartan overlap. The resulting nilpotent quotients carry a well-defined ambient action with a positive trace-form Haar multiplier.
- **claim-strict-width-covering** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-strict-width-tiling)): Fix the cone, radii, and a letter $X_i$ with weight $\alpha\in S$. Fix also $M>0$. For all sufficiently small fixed $\delta>0$, the following claims hold along every sequence $r\to\infty$. Suppose $$k_q=\exp(t_qX_i),\qquad \|t_q\|\le e^{M\delta r+o(r)}.$$ There is a measurable partition of $B^I_r(T_1)$ into sets indexed by anchors $i_\kappa\in B^I_r(T_1)$, with $$\begin{aligned} \text{tile}_\kappa&\subset i_\kappa k_q B^I_r(T_2,O(1))k_q^{-1}, &\#\{\kappa\}&\le e^{r(P_1-P_2)+o(r)}, \label{eq:cone-tiles}\\ k_q^{-1}i_\kappa k_q&\in B^I_r(T_+,O(1)). \label{eq:cone-anchor} \end{aligned}$$ Moreover, if $u\in B_r(T_2,e^{o(r)})$ and $e_r\in N$ satisfies $\\|\log_N e_r\\|\le e^{M\delta r+o(r)}$, the ideal coordinate of $ue_r$ in <a href="#eq:ideal-section-coordinates" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ideal-section-coordinates">[eq:ideal-section-coordinates]</a> belongs to $B^I_r(T_2,e^{o(r)})$. These statements hold simultaneously for any fixed finite collection of the cones, templates, and their images under $\mathcal A$, after decreasing $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Strict gaps between mixed-word widths permit measurable ideal-box coverings and absorb conjugation and bridge errors for sufficiently small fixed chart enlargement.
- **claim-neighboring-affine-offsets** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-neighbor-offsets)): There exists $\delta_2>0$ such that, for every fixed $0<\delta\le\delta_2$, the following holds. Let $r\to\infty$, let $F$ range over the fixed family of smoothed quasi-isometries, let $x\in G$ vary, and let $k_0$ be any allowed deterministic suffix. For a generator letter $X_i$, put $$k=\exp(tX_i),\qquad \|t\|\le e^{(1+\delta)r s_i(\mathcal C)+o(r)}.$$ Every pair in the support of any limiting joint law of $(f_{r,x,ak_0},f_{r,x,ak_0k})$ has identical affine restrictions to $\mathcal U_\delta$. Equivalently, on every fixed compact subset of that domain, the difference of these profiles tends to zero in probability. The conclusion also holds for suffixes and steps sampled independently of $a$, provided their bounds have a common vanishing envelope.
  - Explanation: Cone quotient volume comparisons force neighboring affine offsets to agree, including independently sampled suffixes and steps with a common error envelope.
- **claim-suffix-comparison-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-suffix-concentration)): Let $a$ be uniform on $B_r(\mathcal C)$. Along every sequence $r\to\infty$, with arbitrary maps $F$, basepoints $x$, and suffixes $$k_0\in B_r((1+\delta)\mathcal C,e^{\eta_r r}), \qquad \eta_r\longrightarrow0,$$ one has $$\label{eq:suffix-profile-comparison} \\|f_{r,x,ak_0}-f_{r,x,a}\\|_Z\longrightarrow0 \quad\text{in probability}.$$ The assertion also holds for suffixes sampled independently of $a$ whose supports satisfy the same bound.* *There are deterministic $A_{F,x,r}\in\mathcal A$ and $s_{F,x,r}\in W$ such that $$\label{eq:ordinary-chart-concentration} \sup_{F,x}\mathbb E\left\\| f_{r,x,a}-A_{F,x,r}\pi-\frac{s_{F,x,r}}r \right\\|_Z\longrightarrow0, \qquad \|s_{F,x,r}\|\le C r.$$ The same approximation holds in probability with $a$ replaced by $ak_0$ in the sequential setting of <a href="#eq:suffix-profile-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:suffix-profile-comparison">[eq:suffix-profile-comparison]</a>.
  - Explanation: Allowed suffix changes disappear in probability, and ordinary chart laws concentrate near deterministic affine approximations uniformly over maps and basepoints.
- **claim-scale-stabilization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-scale-stabilization)): There is $R_0$ independent of $F,x$ such that $A_{F,x,r}$ is independent of $r\ge R_0$. Moreover $$\sup_{F,x}\|s_{F,x,r}\|/r\longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: The finite linear label stabilizes at a common large-scale threshold, while deterministic chart offsets become uniformly sublinear.
- **claim-global-uniform-sublinear-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-sublinear)): For each map $F$ there is a single $A_F\in\mathcal A$ and there is a function $\epsilon(R)\to0$, common to the entire fixed family, such that $$\label{eq:uniform-sublinear} \left\|\pi F(xg)-\pi F(x)-A_F\pi g\right\| \le\epsilon(R)R \qquad(F,\ x\in G,\ \|g\|_G\le R).$$ The label of the coarse inverse is $A_{\bar F}=A_F^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: Scale and basepoint comparisons yield a single global height label for each map and a common sublinear error modulus. Coarse inverses have inverse labels.
- **claim-contracting-ray-boundaries** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-ray-boundaries)): Let $F$ belong to the fixed Lipschitz quasi-isometry family, let $A=A_F$, and let $\beta$ be a selected target ray representative. Put $\alpha=\beta\circ A$. There is a homeomorphism $$f_\beta:L_\alpha\longrightarrow L_\beta$$ and a fixed compact set $Q_\beta\subset L_\beta$ such that $$\label{eq:boundary-shadow} f_\beta(p_\alpha x)\in F(x)\cdot Q_\beta \qquad(x\in G).$$ The compact sets may be chosen uniformly over the fixed family. The map obtained from $\bar F$ for the reversed ray pair is $f_\beta^{-1}$.
  - Explanation: Contracting height trajectories define homeomorphisms of ray quotients with uniform compact shadows, and the coarse inverse induces the inverse boundary map.
- **claim-boundary-image-volume** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-volume)): For each source and target ray pair in Lemma <a href="#lem:ray-boundaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ray-boundaries">45</a>, there is a fixed compact ball $U\subset L_\alpha$ with nonempty interior such that $$\label{eq:boundary-volume} \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_{L_\beta}\bigl(f_\beta(x\cdot U)\bigr) \asymp e^{\ell_\beta(\pi F(x))} \qquad(x\in G).$$ The comparison constants and the choices of balls are uniform over the fixed quasi-isometry family.
  - Explanation: Boundary images of a fixed source ball have Haar volume comparable to the exponential of target trace height, uniformly over the map family.
- **claim-bounded-nilpotent-leaf-height** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-leaf-height)): There is a uniform constant $B_N$ such that $$\|\pi F(xn)-\pi F(x)\|\le B_N \qquad(F,\ x\in G,\ n\in N).$$ In particular $\|\pi F(nd)-\pi F(d)\|\le B_N$ for all $n\in N,d\in D$.
  - Explanation: Boundary image volumes and the positive relation among generator rays bound height variation along every nilpotent fiber, independently of its displacement.
- **claim-haar-translate-averaging** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-haar-averaging)): Let $L$ be a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group with Haar measure $\mu$ and a left invariant Riemannian metric. Let $\rho$ be a Radon measure on $L$. Suppose a family of compact positive-Haar-measure sets $E_j$, including $E_0$, and positive numbers $a_j$ satisfy $$K^{-1}a_j\le\rho(zE_j)\le Ka_j \qquad(z\in L,\ j)$$ for one $K\ge1$. Then $$\frac{a_j}{\mu(E_j)}\asymp \frac{a_0}{\mu(E_0)}$$ with constants depending only on $K$ and a large-scale volume doubling constant of $L$, independently of the shapes and diameters of the sets. Absolute continuity of $\rho$ is not required.
  - Explanation: Uniform translate-mass comparisons force mass-to-Haar-volume ratios to be comparable independently of the shapes of the compact sets, even for singular Radon measures.
- **claim-comparison-lie-models** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-model-reduction)): Let $P$ be a finitely generated virtually polycyclic group, and let $H$ be a finitely generated group quasi-isometric to $P$. There exist a finite-index subgroup $\Gamma\leq P$, a connected simply connected solvable Lie group $S$, and a connected simply connected real-triangulable unimodular Lie group $G$ such that $$\Gamma\text{ is a uniform lattice in }S, \qquad H\simeq_{\mathrm{QI}}P\simeq_{\mathrm{QI}}S \simeq_{\mathrm{QI}}G.$$ The group $H$ is amenable. Moreover, $\Gamma$ has a finite classifying space and is an integral Poincaré duality group of dimension $\dim S$.
  - Explanation: A finite-index comparison lattice supplies finiteness and duality, while a quasi-isometric real-triangulable unimodular model supplies height geometry. Amenability transfers to the comparison group.
- **claim-height-kernel-polynomial-growth** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-height-kernel-growth)): Let $G$ be a connected simply connected real-triangulable unimodular Lie group with the height homomorphism $\pi:G\to W$, and let $f:H\to G$ be a quasi-isometry from a finitely generated group, normalized by $f(e)=1$. Suppose $H_0\leq H$ and $\tau:H_0\to W$ is a homomorphism such that, for some $D<\infty$, $$\label{eq:recognition-corrected-height} \\|\pi f(h)-\tau(h)\\|\leq D\qquad(h\in H_0).$$ Then every finitely generated subgroup of $K_0=\ker\tau$ has polynomial growth and is virtually nilpotent.
  - Explanation: A uniformly corrected height homomorphism gives bounded-height word paths in each finitely generated kernel subgroup, proving intrinsic polynomial growth and virtual nilpotence without an undistortion assumption.
- **claim-elementary-amenability-from-height** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-recognition-from-height)): Let $G$ be a connected simply connected real-triangulable unimodular Lie group with height homomorphism $\pi:G\to W$. Let $H$ be a finitely generated amenable group quasi-isometric to $G$. Assume the uniform bounded-height conclusion of Theorem <a href="#thm:bounded-height" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:bounded-height">4</a> for the family of self-quasi-isometries transferred from left translations of $H$. Then there is a finite-index normal subgroup $H_0\leq H$ and a homomorphism $\tau:H_0\to W$ whose kernel is locally virtually nilpotent. In particular, $H$ is elementary amenable.
  - Explanation: The finite linear height action is removed by passing to its kernel. An invariant mean corrects the bounded translation defect, and locally virtually nilpotent kernel subgroups imply elementary amenability.

        "exact_quote": "There is a real generalized weight decomposition $$\\label{eq:cartan-weight-decomposition}\n \\mathfrak g=\\bigoplus_\\gamma\\mathfrak g_\\gamma,\n \\qquad \\mathfrak g_0=\\mathfrak d,\n \\qquad\n [\\mathfrak g_\\gamma,\\mathfrak g_\\eta]\n       \\subseteq\\mathfrak g_{\\gamma+\\eta}.$$ Let $\\mathfrak n$ be the subalgebra generated by the spaces with nonzero weight. Then $\\mathfrak n$ is a nilpotent ideal contained in $[\\mathfrak g,\\mathfrak g]$, and $\\mathfrak g=\\mathfrak n+\\mathfrak d$. There are homogeneous generators $X_1,\\ldots,X_\\nu$ of $\\mathfrak n$ whose images form a basis of $\\mathfrak n/[\\mathfrak n,\\mathfrak n]$ and whose weights $\\alpha_1,\\ldots,\\alpha_\\nu$ are all nonzero.*\n\n\n*The groups $N=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak n)$ and $D=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak d)$ are closed and simply connected, and $N$ is normal in $G$. With the conjugation action of $D$ on $N$, multiplication defines a surjective Lie homomorphism and submersion $$\\label{eq:cartan-multiplication}\n q:N\\rtimes D\\longrightarrow G,\\qquad q(n,d)=nd.$$ Its kernel is $$\\label{eq:cartan-overlap-kernel}\n \\ker q=\\{(a^{-1},a):a\\in N\\cap D\\}.$$ Moreover, $N\\cap D=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak d)$. Every compact subset of $G$ has lifts in a compact subset of $N\\rtimes D$. Finally, $N=1$ if and only if $G$ is nilpotent.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cartan-model",
          "exact_quote": "There is a real generalized weight decomposition $$\\label{eq:cartan-weight-decomposition}\n \\mathfrak g=\\bigoplus_\\gamma\\mathfrak g_\\gamma,\n \\qquad \\mathfrak g_0=\\mathfrak d,\n \\qquad\n [\\mathfrak g_\\gamma,\\mathfrak g_\\eta]\n       \\subseteq\\mathfrak g_{\\gamma+\\eta}.$$ Let $\\mathfrak n$ be the subalgebra generated by the spaces with nonzero weight. Then $\\mathfrak n$ is a nilpotent ideal contained in $[\\mathfrak g,\\mathfrak g]$, and $\\mathfrak g=\\mathfrak n+\\mathfrak d$. There are homogeneous generators $X_1,\\ldots,X_\\nu$ of $\\mathfrak n$ whose images form a basis of $\\mathfrak n/[\\mathfrak n,\\mathfrak n]$ and whose weights $\\alpha_1,\\ldots,\\alpha_\\nu$ are all nonzero.*\n\n\n*The groups $N=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak n)$ and $D=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak d)$ are closed and simply connected, and $N$ is normal in $G$. With the conjugation action of $D$ on $N$, multiplication defines a surjective Lie homomorphism and submersion $$\\label{eq:cartan-multiplication}\n q:N\\rtimes D\\longrightarrow G,\\qquad q(n,d)=nd.$$ Its kernel is $$\\label{eq:cartan-overlap-kernel}\n \\ker q=\\{(a^{-1},a):a\\in N\\cap D\\}.$$ Moreover, $N\\cap D=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak d)$.       "explanation": "Generalized Cartan weights produce a nilpotent normal factor and a nilpotent Cartan factor whose multiplication surjects onto the ambient group, retaining their overlap.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-d-scaling",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C\\ge1$, depending only on $D$, the metric, and the splitting, such that for $d=\\exp_D(\\sum_jv_j)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|v_j\\|&\\le C(1+|d|_D)^j \\quad(1\\le j\\le s),\n       \\label{eq:d-coordinate-upper}\\\\\n |d|_D&\\le C\\left(1+\\max_{1\\le j\\le s}\\|v_j\\|^{1/j}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:d-coordinate-reverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The group laws transported from $D$ by $\\Delta_r$ converge, with all derivatives on compact coordinate sets, to the law of $D_\\infty$ as $r\\to\\infty$. Every compact $K\\subset D_\\infty$ satisfies $\\sup_{z\\in K}|\\Delta_r z|_D=O_K(r)$ for $r\\ge1$. Conversely, for every fixed $C_0$, all $\\Delta_r^{-1}\\{d:|d|_D\\le C_0r\\}$, $r\\ge1$, lie in one compact coordinate set. If $z_r,z'_r$ belong to a fixed compact coordinate set and their coordinate difference tends to zero, then $$\\label{eq:d-scaling-equicontinuity}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_D(\\Delta_r z_r,\\Delta_r z'_r)=o(r).$$ For $v\\in V_1$ one also has $$\\label{eq:d-horizontal-flow-limit}\n \\Delta_r^{-1}\\bigl(\\Delta_r z\\exp_D(rt v)\\bigr)\n     \\longrightarrow z\\exp_\\infty(tv),$$ uniformly when $z,t,v$ range over fixed compact sets. Haar measures on $D$ and $D_\\infty$ are Lebesgue measure in these exponential coordinates, up to fixed scalars. If both scalars are chosen equal and $Q=\\sum_jj\\dim V_j$, then $\\Delta_r$ multiplies Haar volume by $r^Q$, and $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-volume}\n c t^Q\\le \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D\\{d:|d|_D\\le t\\}\\le C t^Q \\qquad(t\\ge1)$$ for some $c>0$. In particular $D$ has doubling Haar measure: $$\\label{eq:nilpotent-doubling}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,2t)\\le C\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,t)\\qquad(t>0).$$ Every conclusion of this lemma holds for an arbitrary connected, simply connected nilpotent Lie group $L$ equipped with a left invariant Riemannian metric and a splitting of its lower central filtration, with $L$ in place of $D$ and constants allowed to depend on $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-d-scaling",

Every compact $K\\subset D_\\infty$ satisfies $\\sup_{z\\in K}|\\Delta_r z|_D=O_K(r)$ for $r\\ge1$. Conversely, for every fixed $C_0$, all $\\Delta_r^{-1}\\{d:|d|_D\\le C_0r\\}$, $r\\ge1$, lie in one compact coordinate set. Haar measures on $D$ and $D_\\infty$ are Lebesgue measure in these exponential coordinates, up to fixed scalars. If both scalars are chosen equal and $Q=\\sum_jj\\dim V_j$, then $\\Delta_r$ multiplies Haar volume by $r^Q$, and $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-volume}\n c t^Q\\le \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D\\{d:|d|_D\\le t\\}\\le C t^Q \\qquad(t\\ge1)$$ for some $c>0$. In particular $D$ has doubling Haar measure: $$\\label{eq:nilpotent-doubling}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,2t)\\le C\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,t)\\qquad(t>0).$$ Every conclusion of this lemma holds for an arbitrary connected, simply connected nilpotent Lie group $L$ equipped with a left invariant Riemannian metric and a splitting of its lower central filtration, with $L$ in place of $D$ and constants allowed to depend on $L$.",

      "exact_statement": "There is a constant $C\\ge1$, depending only on $D$, the metric, and the splitting, such that for $d=\\exp_D(\\sum_jv_j)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|v_j\\|&\\le C(1+|d|_D)^j \\quad(1\\le j\\le s),\n       \\label{eq:d-coordinate-upper}\\\\\n |d|_D&\\le C\\left(1+\\max_{1\\le j\\le s}\\|v_j\\|^{1/j}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:d-coordinate-reverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The group laws transported from $D$ by $\\Delta_r$ converge, with all derivatives on compact coordinate sets, to the law of $D_\\infty$ as $r\\to\\infty$. Every compact $K\\subset D_\\infty$ satisfies $\\sup_{z\\in K}|\\Delta_r z|_D=O_K(r)$ for $r\\ge1$. If both scalars are chosen equal and $Q=\\sum_jj\\dim V_j$, then $\\Delta_r$ multiplies Haar volume by $r^Q$, and $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-volume}\n c t^Q\\le \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D\\{d:|d|_D\\le t\\}\\le C t^Q \\qquad(t\\ge1)$$ for some $c>0$. In particular $D$ has doubling Haar measure: $$\\label{eq:nilpotent-doubling}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,2t)\\le C\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,t)\\qquad(t>0).$$ Every conclusion of this lemma holds for an arbitrary connected, simply connected nilpotent Lie group $L$ equipped with a left invariant Riemannian metric and a splitting of its lower central filtration, with $L$ in place of $D$ and constants allowed to depend on $L$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-canonical-height",
        "exact_quote": "Each generator weight $\\alpha_i$ vanishes on $[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d]+(\\mathfrak d\\cap\\mathfrak n)$, and inclusion of $\\mathfrak d$ into $\\mathfrak g$ induces an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:canonical-cartan-height}\n W\\cong V\\Big/\\bigcap_{i=1}^\\nu\\ker\\alpha_i,\n \\qquad V=\\mathfrak d/[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d].$$ Under this identification, $\\pi$ kills $N$ and its restriction to $D$ is the displayed quotient of its abelianization. When $\\nu=0$, the intersection on the right is all of $V$, and $W=0$; this occurs exactly when $G$ is nilpotent.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathfrak n_\\gamma=\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak g_\\gamma$ and view every weight occurring in $\\mathfrak n$ as a linear form on $W$. For fixed norms, there are constants $C,M$ such that, for every $d\\in D$ and every such weight $\\gamma$, $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-unipotent}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}\n     =e^{\\gamma(\\pi d)}U_\\gamma(d),\\qquad\n \\|U_\\gamma(d)\\|+\\|U_\\gamma(d)^{-1}\\|\n     \\le C(1+|d|_D)^M,$$ where $U_\\gamma(d)$ is unipotent. For $w\\in W$, the linear map $$\\label{eq:exact-weight-dilation}\n S_w|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}=e^{\\gamma(w)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$$ is a Lie algebra automorphism and hence a group automorphism of $N$. Both $S_w$ and conjugation by $d$ preserve Haar measure on $N$. Consequently product Haar measure $dn\\,dd$ is Haar measure on $N\\rtimes D$.*\n\n\n*The height map on $D_\\infty$ is the homomorphism induced by $V_1\\cong V\\to W$, and it satisfies $$\\label{eq:d-height-scaling}\n \\pi(\\Delta_r z)=r\\pi(z)\n \\qquad(z\\in D_\\infty, r>0).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-canonical-height",
          "exact_quote": "Each generator weight $\\alpha_i$ vanishes on $[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d]+(\\mathfrak d\\cap\\mathfrak n)$, and inclusion of $\\mathfrak d$ into $\\mathfrak g$ induces an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:canonical-cartan-height}\n W\\cong V\\Big/\\bigcap_{i=1}^\\nu\\ker\\alpha_i,\n \\qquad V=\\mathfrak d/[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d].$$ Under this identification, $\\pi$ kills $N$ and its restriction to $D$ is the displayed quotient of its abelianization. When $\\nu=0$, the intersection on the right is all of $V$, and $W=0$; this occurs exactly when $G$ is nilpotent.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathfrak n_\\gamma=\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak g_\\gamma$ and view every weight occurring in $\\mathfrak n$ as a linear form on $W$. For fixed norms, there are constants $C,M$ such that, for every $d\\in D$ and every such weight $\\gamma$, $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-unipotent}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}\n     =e^{\\gamma(\\pi d)}U_\\gamma(d),\\qquad\n \\|U_\\gamma(d)\\|+\\|U_\\gamma(d)^{-1}\\|\n     \\le C(1+|d|_D)^M,$$ where $U_\\gamma(d)$ is unipotent. For $w\\in W$, the linear map $$\\label{eq:exact-weight-dilation}\n S_w|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}=e^{\\gamma(w)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$$ is a Lie algebra automorphism and hence a group automorphism of $N$. Both $S_w$ and conjugation by $d$ preserve Haar measure on $N$. Consequently product Haar measure $dn\\,dd$ is Haar measure on $N\\rtimes D$.*\n\n\n*The height map on $D_\\infty$ is the homomorphism induced by $V_1\\cong V\\to W$, and it satisfies $$\\label{eq:d-height-scaling}\n \\pi(\\Delta_r z)=r\\pi(z)\n \\qquad(z\\in D_\\infty, r>0).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Each generator weight $\\alpha_i$ vanishes on $[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d]+(\\mathfrak d\\cap\\mathfrak n)$, and inclusion of $\\mathfrak d$ into $\\mathfrak g$ induces an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:canonical-cartan-height}\n W\\cong V\\Big/\\bigcap_{i=1}^\\nu\\ker\\alpha_i,\n \\qquad V=\\mathfrak d/[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d].$$ Under this identification, $\\pi$ kills $N$ and its restriction to $D$ is the displayed quotient of its abelianization. When $\\nu=0$, the intersection on the right is all of $V$, and $W=0$; this occurs exactly when $G$ is nilpotent.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathfrak n_\\gamma=\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak g_\\gamma$ and view every weight occurring in $\\mathfrak n$ as a linear form on $W$. For fixed norms, there are constants $C,M$ such that, for every $d\\in D$ and every such weight $\\gamma$, $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-unipotent}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}\n     =e^{\\gamma(\\pi d)}U_\\gamma(d),\\qquad\n \\|U_\\gamma(d)\\|+\\|U_\\gamma(d)^{-1}\\|\n     \\le C(1+|d|_D)^M,$$ where $U_\\gamma(d)$ is unipotent. For $w\\in W$, the linear map $$\\label{eq:exact-weight-dilation}\n S_w|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}=e^{\\gamma(w)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$$ is a Lie algebra automorphism and hence a group automorphism of $N$. Both $S_w$ and conjugation by $d$ preserve Haar measure on $N$. Consequently product Haar measure $dn\\,dd$ is Haar measure on $N\\rtimes D$.*\n\n\n*The height map on $D_\\infty$ is the homomorphism induced by $V_1\\cong V\\to W$, and it satisfies $$\\label{eq:d-height-scaling}\n \\pi(\\Delta_r z)=r\\pi(z)\n \\qquad(z\\in D_\\infty, r>0).$$",
      "explanation": "The canonical height quotient is recovered from generator weights on the Cartan abelianization. Conjugation separates exponential scalar factors from polynomial unipotent factors, and the relevant actions preserve Haar measure.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-box-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants depending only on the fixed Lie model with the following properties, uniformly for $r\\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$.*\n\n1.  *The unit cube in a maximizing scaled basis and the Minkowski sum of the intervals $[-e^{rh_Z(E)}Z,e^{rh_Z(E)}Z]$, $Z\\in\\mathcal Z$, contain constant multiples of one another. Different maximizing bases therefore give boxes comparable by fixed changes of $L$.*\n\n2.  *In these coordinates all Lie bracket coefficients are bounded uniformly. Multiplication and inversion in exponential coordinates are polynomials of uniformly bounded degree and coefficients. For some $C,b$, a product of $k\\ge1$ elements of $B_r(E,L)$ lies in $$B_r(E,C(1+k)^b(1+L)^b).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $d_N=\\dim N$, Haar measure satisfies $$\\label{eq:box-volume}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_N B_r(E,L)\\asymp L^{d_N}e^{rP(E)}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *Exact weight dilations shift the template: $S_{rw}B_r(E,L)$ is comparable, with fixed radius factors, to $B_r(E+w,L)$. For each fixed $C_0$ there are $C,b$ such that, for $r\\ge1$ and $|d|_D\\le C_0r$, $$\\label{eq:box-conjugation}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d B_r(E,L)\\subseteq\n     B_r\\left(E+\\frac{\\pi d}{r},C(1+r)^b(1+L)^b\\right).$$ The same assertion applies to $d^{-1}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-box-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants depending only on the fixed Lie model with the following properties, uniformly for $r\\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$.*\n\n1.  *The unit cube in a maximizing scaled basis and the Minkowski sum of the intervals $[-e^{rh_Z(E)}Z,e^{rh_Z(E)}Z]$, $Z\\in\\mathcal Z$, contain constant multiples of one another. Different maximizing bases therefore give boxes comparable by fixed changes of $L$.*\n\n2.  *In these coordinates all Lie bracket coefficients are bounded uniformly. Multiplication and inversion in exponential coordinates are polynomials of uniformly bounded degree and coefficients. For some $C,b$, a product of $k\\ge1$ elements of $B_r(E,L)$ lies in $$B_r(E,C(1+k)^b(1+L)^b).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $d_N=\\dim N$, Haar measure satisfies $$\\label{eq:box-volume}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_N B_r(E,L)\\asymp L^{d_N}e^{rP(E)}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *Exact weight dilations shift the template: $S_{rw}B_r(E,L)$ is comparable, with fixed radius factors, to $B_r(E+w,L)$. For each fixed $C_0$ there are $C,b$ such that, for $r\\ge1$ and $|d|_D\\le C_0r$, $$\\label{eq:box-conjugation}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d B_r(E,L)\\subseteq\n     B_r\\left(E+\\frac{\\pi d}{r},C(1+r)^b(1+L)^b\\right).$$ The same assertion applies to $d^{-1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There are constants depending only on the fixed Lie model with the following properties, uniformly for $r\\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$.*\n\n1.  *The unit cube in a maximizing scaled basis and the Minkowski sum of the intervals $[-e^{rh_Z(E)}Z,e^{rh_Z(E)}Z]$, $Z\\in\\mathcal Z$, contain constant multiples of one another. Different maximizing bases therefore give boxes comparable by fixed changes of $L$.*\n\n2.  *In these coordinates all Lie bracket coefficients are bounded uniformly. Multiplication and inversion in exponential coordinates are polynomials of uniformly bounded degree and coefficients. For some $C,b$, a product of $k\\ge1$ elements of $B_r(E,L)$ lies in $$B_r(E,C(1+k)^b(1+L)^b).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $d_N=\\dim N$, Haar measure satisfies $$\\label{eq:box-volume}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_N B_r(E,L)\\asymp L^{d_N}e^{rP(E)}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *Exact weight dilations shift the template: $S_{rw}B_r(E,L)$ is comparable, with fixed radius factors, to $B_r(E+w,L)$. For each fixed $C_0$ there are $C,b$ such that, for $r\\ge1$ and $|d|_D\\le C_0r$, $$\\label{eq:box-conjugation}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d B_r(E,L)\\subseteq\n     B_r\\left(E+\\frac{\\pi d}{r},C(1+r)^b(1+L)^b\\right).$$ The same assertion applies to $d^{-1}$.",

        "exact_quote": "The function $P$ is translation invariant, depends only on the convex hull, and is Lipschitz for Hausdorff distance.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-product-schemes",
        "exact_quote": "For each fixed $L<\\infty$ there are integers and constants depending only on $L$ and the Lie model such that every element of $B_r(E,L)$, for every $r\\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$, is the endpoint of a scheme of at most that many factors, with all $|t_j|$ bounded by that constant. Choices may be made Borel measurably in the endpoint.*\n\n*For any sequence of these normalized coordinate systems there is a subsequence and one fixed generator product scheme whose normalized polynomial maps converge, with bounded degree and coefficients, to a map submersive at an interior point of a fixed parameter cube. The parameter point may be chosen to map to the identity.*\n\n*If $r\\to\\infty$ and the radius is $e^{o(r)}$, generation still uses a bounded number of factors, now with amplitudes $e^{rs_i(E)+o(r)}$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-switch-paths",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a compact convex $E_V\\subset V$, a point $v_0\\in E_V$, a constant $L$, and pivots $d_0\\in D$ satisfying $a_D(d_0)=rv_0$ and $|d_0|_D\\le C_0r$, where $r\\ge1$. Every $u\\in B_r(\\pi E_V,L)$ can be reached from $d_0$ at endpoint $ud_0$ by a path with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *it uses a bounded number of $D$-legs and generator switches, has length at most $Cr$, and all its $D$-positions have intrinsic length at most $Cr$;*\n\n2.  *on each $D$-leg the abelianized position lies in $rE_V$; each connecting switch has length at most $C\\log(2+r)$;*\n\n3.  *for any fixed continuous homomorphism $l:G\\to W$, all path values of $l$ lie within $O_l(\\log(2+r))$ of $r\\,l(E_V)$, where $l$ on $V$ denotes its induced linear map.*\n\n*Choices may be made measurably in $u$. The analogous statements with switch length $o(r)$ hold for a subexponential box radius. In particular, for a fixed compact convex $E\\subset W$ and any pivot of intrinsic length at most $C_0r$ with $\\pi d_0\\in rE$, the same construction reaches $ud_0$, $u\\in B_r(E,L)$, with absolute heights in $rE+O(\\log(2+r))$ and total length $O(r)$. The constants in this specialization are independent of the pivot’s other abelianized coordinates. One may also use a prescribed $D$-curve of length $O(r)$ and intrinsic positions $O(r)$ whose height image attains all the required supports, by visiting and reversing portions of that curve.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-packing",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0>0$ and a separation distance $a>0$. There are constants $C,b$ such that the following holds for $r\\ge1$. Let $E\\subset W$ be compact and contain zero. The endpoints of paths starting at one point $x$, of length at most $C_0r$, and satisfying $$\\pi(x^{-1}\\gamma(t))\\in rE\n \\quad\\hbox{at every point of each path}$$ have $a$-separated subsets of cardinality at most $$\\label{eq:finite-path-packing}\n C(1+r)^b e^{rP(E)}.$$ Each endpoint has a relative lift $(u,d)$ with $$u\\in B_r(E,C(1+r)^b),\\qquad |d|_D\\le Cr.$$ These containments remain valid, after increasing $C,b$, under fixed bounded right thickening in $N\\rtimes D$.*\n\n*In particular, along a sequence with relative path heights in $r(E+o(1))$ and length $O(r)$, the upper separated count is $e^{rP(E)+o(r)}$, and the lifts have $u\\in B_r(E,e^{o(r)})$, $|d|_D=O(r)$. Without a height restriction, a path of length $t$ has a relative lift with $|d|_D\\le C(1+t)$ and $\\|\\log_Nu\\|\\le e^{C(1+t)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-packing",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0>0$ and a separation distance $a>0$. There are constants $C,b$ such that the following holds for $r\\ge1$. Let $E\\subset W$ be compact and contain zero. Without a height restriction, a path of length $t$ has a relative lift with $|d|_D\\le C(1+t)$ and $\\|\\log_Nu\\|\\le e^{C(1+t)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix $C_0>0$ and a separation distance $a>0$. There are constants $C,b$ such that the following holds for $r\\ge1$. Let $E\\subset W$ be compact and contain zero.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-fixed-sheet-packing",
        "exact_quote": "For every $a>0$ there is $C_a<\\infty$ such that, for every $d\\in D$, $x\\in G$, and bounded measurable $S\\subset N$, the set $\\{xnd:n\\in S\\}$ contains an $a$-separated subset with cardinality at least $\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_N(S)/C_a$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-lift-multiplicity",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a radius $R$. There are $C,b,c$ such that, for $T,H\\ge0$ and any metric ball $B_G(y,R)$, the set $$\\{u\\in N:\\text{ for some }d\\in D, |d|_D\\le T,\n \\ \\|\\pi d\\|\\le H,\\ q(u,d)\\in B_G(y,R)\\}$$ has $N$-Haar outer measure at most $C(1+T)^b e^{cH}$. In particular, if $T=O(r)$ and $H=o(r)$, this measure is $e^{o(r)}$, with one common envelope for any family subject to those bounds.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-image",
        "exact_quote": "Fix integers $a\\ge b\\ge1$ and $D\\ge1$. There are constants $C>0$ and $A_1,A_2>0$, depending only on $a,b,D$, with the following property. Let $\\Phi:\\mathbb R^a\\to\\mathbb R^b$ be a polynomial map of degree at most $D$, all of whose coefficients have absolute value at most $B\\ge0$. Suppose that one $b$-column minor of $D\\Phi$ has a coefficient of absolute value at least $c>0$. Put $c_0=\\min\\{c,1\\}$. For every $R\\ge1$ and every Lebesgue-measurable $E\\subset[-R,R]^a$ of measure $m>0$, $$\\label{eq:polynomial-image-quantitative}\n |\\Phi(E)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\n \\ge C c_0^{b+1}(1+B)^{-b^2}\n          m^{A_1}(1+R)^{-A_2}.$$ One may take, with $d=\\max\\{1,b(D-1)\\}$, $$A_1=1+ad(b+1),\\qquad\n A_2=a+a^2d(b+1)+Db^2.$$*\n\n*In particular, let $r\\to\\infty$ along a sequence and let $\\Phi_r$ have these fixed dimensions and degree bound. Suppose their coefficients converge to those of a polynomial map which is submersive at some point. If the $E_r$ are Lebesgue-measurable and $$E_r\\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a,\\qquad\n 1\\le R_r\\le e^{o(r)},\\qquad |E_r|\\ge e^{-o(r)},$$ then $$\\label{eq:polynomial-image-subexponential}\n |\\Phi_r(E_r)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ The same conclusion follows from <a href=\"#eq:polynomial-image-quantitative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:polynomial-image-quantitative\">[eq:polynomial-image-quantitative]</a> if the coefficient upper bounds $B_r$ and a chosen minor-coefficient lower bound $c_r$ satisfy $\\log(1+B_r)+\\log(1/\\min\\{c_r,1\\})=o(r)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-image",

      "exact_statement": "Fix integers $a\\ge b\\ge1$ and $D\\ge1$. There are constants $C>0$ and $A_1,A_2>0$, depending only on $a,b,D$, with the following property.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-backward-recurrence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J$ be a second countable unimodular locally compact group with Haar measure $\\mu$. Let $Q\\subset J$ be compact with $0<\\mu(Q)<\\infty$, and let $S\\subset Q$ be Borel with $\\mu(S)>0$. Fix an integer $h\\ge1$ and compactly supported probability measures $\\sigma_1,\\ldots,\\sigma_h$ on $J$. Suppose that numbers $D_j\\ge1$ satisfy $$\\mu(Q\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma_j)\\le D_j\\mu(Q)\n       \\qquad(1\\le j\\le h).$$ There are nested Borel sets $$S_0\\subset S_1\\subset\\cdots\\subset S_h=S$$ with positive measure such that, writing $a_j=\\mu(S_j)/\\mu(Q)$ and $\\tau_j=a_j/(2D_j)$, one has $$\\label{eq:recurrence-mass}\n a_{j-1}\\ge\\frac{a_j^2}{2D_j}$$ and $$\\label{eq:recurrence-transition}\n \\mathbb P\\{x u_j\\in S_j\\}\\ge\\tau_j\n       \\qquad(x\\in S_{j-1}),$$ where $u_j$ has law $\\sigma_j*\\check\\sigma_j$. If the $u_j$ are sampled independently, every $x\\in S_0$ therefore has probability at least $\\prod_{j=1}^h\\tau_j$ of satisfying $$x u_1\\cdots u_j\\in S_j\\subset S\n       \\qquad(1\\le j\\le h).$$ In a sequence indexed by $r\\to\\infty$, if $h$ is fixed, $\\mu(S)/\\mu(Q)\\ge e^{-o(r)}$, and every $D_j\\le e^{o(r)}$, then $\\mu(S_0)/\\mu(Q)$ and the displayed success probability are at least $e^{-o(r)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-backward-recurrence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $J$ be a second countable unimodular locally compact group with Haar measure $\\mu$. If the $u_j$ are sampled independently, every $x\\in S_0$ therefore has probability at least $\\prod_{j=1}^h\\tau_j$ of satisfying $$x u_1\\cdots u_j\\in S_j\\subset S\n       \\qquad(1\\le j\\le h).$$ In a sequence indexed by $r\\to\\infty$, if $h$ is fixed, $\\mu(S)/\\mu(Q)\\ge e^{-o(r)}$, and every $D_j\\le e^{o(r)}$, then $\\mu(S_0)/\\mu(Q)$ and the displayed success probability are at least $e^{-o(r)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $J$ be a second countable unimodular locally compact group with Haar measure $\\mu$.       "explanation": "Backward selection retains nested positive-measure starting sets and lower transition probabilities, allowing a fixed sequence of independent switches to remain in a prescribed Borel set.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-restricted-products",
        "exact_quote": "Let $r\\to\\infty$ along a sequence. Fix $h\\ge1$, positive integers $a_1,\\ldots,a_h$, and $a=\\sum_j a_j$. For each $r$, let $J_r,Q_r,S_r,\\mu_r$, and $\\sigma_{j,r}$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:backward-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:backward-recurrence\">18</a>, with $$\\frac{\\mu_r(S_r)}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\mu_r(Q_r\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma_{j,r})}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\n       \\le e^{o(r)}.$$ For each $j$, let $\\theta_j\\in\\mathbb R^{a_j}$ have a probability law $\\nu_{j,r}$, and let the $\\theta_j$ be independent. Suppose their joint law $\\nu_r$ gives full measure to a Borel admissible domain $\\Theta_r\\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a$, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure, and satisfies $$1\\le R_r\\le e^{o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{d\\nu_r}{d\\theta}\\le e^{o(r)}\n       \\quad\\text{almost everywhere}.$$ Let $v_{j,r}:\\mathbb R^{a_j}\\to J_r$ be Borel maps whose images of the laws $\\nu_{j,r}$ are $\\sigma_{j,r}*\\check\\sigma_{j,r}$. Finally, let $\\Phi_r:\\mathbb R^a\\to\\mathbb R^b$, $1\\le b\\le a$, be polynomial maps of a fixed degree bound whose coefficients converge to a map submersive somewhere.*\n\n*There is a starting point $x_r\\in S_r$ such that the successful parameter set $$E_r=\\left\\{\\theta\\in\\Theta_r:\n x_r v_{1,r}(\\theta_1)\\cdots v_{j,r}(\\theta_j)\\in S_r\n \\text{ for every }1\\le j\\le h\\right\\}$$ satisfies $$|E_r|\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n |\\Phi_r(E_r)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ Here $\\Phi_r$ may record a product endpoint, several successive endpoints, or a tuple of bundle products; it need not record the intermediate states used in the definition of $E_r$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-restricted-products",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r\\to\\infty$ along a sequence. Fix $h\\ge1$, positive integers $a_1,\\ldots,a_h$, and $a=\\sum_j a_j$. For each $r$, let $J_r,Q_r,S_r,\\mu_r$, and $\\sigma_{j,r}$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:backward-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:backward-recurrence\">18</a>, with $$\\frac{\\mu_r(S_r)}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\mu_r(Q_r\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma_{j,r})}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\n       \\le e^{o(r)}.$$ For each $j$, let $\\theta_j\\in\\mathbb R^{a_j}$ have a probability law $\\nu_{j,r}$, and let the $\\theta_j$ be independent. Suppose their joint law $\\nu_r$ gives full measure to a Borel admissible domain $\\Theta_r\\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a$, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure, and satisfies $$1\\le R_r\\le e^{o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{d\\nu_r}{d\\theta}\\le e^{o(r)}\n       \\quad\\text{almost everywhere}.$$ Let $v_{j,r}:\\mathbb R^{a_j}\\to J_r$ be Borel maps whose images of the laws $\\nu_{j,r}$ are $\\sigma_{j,r}*\\check\\sigma_{j,r}$. Finally, let $\\Phi_r:\\mathbb R^a\\to\\mathbb R^b$, $1\\le b\\le a$, be polynomial maps of a fixed degree bound whose coefficients converge to a map submersive somewhere.*\n\n*There is a starting point $x_r\\in S_r$ such that the successful parameter set $$E_r=\\left\\{\\theta\\in\\Theta_r:\n x_r v_{1,r}(\\theta_1)\\cdots v_{j,r}(\\theta_j)\\in S_r\n \\text{ for every }1\\le j\\le h\\right\\}$$ satisfies $$|E_r|\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n |\\Phi_r(E_r)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ Here $\\Phi_r$ may record a product endpoint, several successive endpoints, or a tuple of bundle products; it need not record the intermediate states used in the definition of $E_r$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $r\\to\\infty$ along a sequence. Fix $h\\ge1$, positive integers $a_1,\\ldots,a_h$, and $a=\\sum_j a_j$. For each $r$, let $J_r,Q_r,S_r,\\mu_r$, and $\\sigma_{j,r}$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:backward-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:backward-recurrence\">18</a>, with $$\\frac{\\mu_r(S_r)}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\mu_r(Q_r\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma_{j,r})}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\n       \\le e^{o(r)}.$$ For each $j$, let $\\theta_j\\in\\mathbb R^{a_j}$ have a probability law $\\nu_{j,r}$, and let the $\\theta_j$ be independent. Suppose their joint law $\\nu_r$ gives full measure to a Borel admissible domain $\\Theta_r\\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a$, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure, and satisfies $$1\\le R_r\\le e^{o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{d\\nu_r}{d\\theta}\\le e^{o(r)}\n       \\quad\\text{almost everywhere}.$$ Let $v_{j,r}:\\mathbb R^{a_j}\\to J_r$ be Borel maps whose images of the laws $\\nu_{j,r}$ are $\\sigma_{j,r}*\\check\\sigma_{j,r}$. Finally, let $\\Phi_r:\\mathbb R^a\\to\\mathbb R^b$, $1\\le b\\le a$, be polynomial maps of a fixed degree bound whose coefficients converge to a map submersive somewhere.*\n\n*There is a starting point $x_r\\in S_r$ such that the successful parameter set $$E_r=\\left\\{\\theta\\in\\Theta_r:\n x_r v_{1,r}(\\theta_1)\\cdots v_{j,r}(\\theta_j)\\in S_r\n \\text{ for every }1\\le j\\le h\\right\\}$$ satisfies $$|E_r|\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n |\\Phi_r(E_r)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ Here $\\Phi_r$ may record a product endpoint, several successive endpoints, or a tuple of bundle products; it need not record the intermediate states used in the definition of $E_r$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-lipschitz-pairs",
        "exact_quote": "For each $K\\ge1$ and $C\\ge0$, there are constants $B<\\infty$ and $\\Lambda\\ge1$, depending only on $G,K,C$, with the following property. Every $(K,C)$ self quasi-isometry of $G$ is within distance $B$ of a map $F$ belonging to a pair $(F,\\bar F)$ such that both maps are $\\Lambda$-Lipschitz, both satisfy $$d(J(g),J(h))\\ge \\Lambda^{-1}d(g,h)-\\Lambda\n \\qquad(J=F,\\bar F),$$ and $$d(\\bar F(F(g)),g)\\le\\Lambda,\\qquad\n d(F(\\bar F(g)),g)\\le\\Lambda\n \\qquad(g\\in G).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lipschitz-pairs",

          "source_id": "PM-255.1"

        "exact_quote": "The spaces $X$ and $Y$ are compact metrizable.         "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be an invariant ergodic probability on $X$. For each fixed $0<R<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:stationary-sublinear}\n \\lim_{r\\to\\infty}\\frac1r\\sup_{|x|_G\\le Rr}\n \\|\\pi F(x)-l(x)\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}=0.$$ These assertions concern the fixed measure $\\mu$; no rate uniform over invariant measures is asserted.",
