        "anchor": "stmt-lem-inverse-coupling",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be an invariant probability on $X$ and let $A\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}(W)$. Suppose there are $\\phi_j\\in L^2(X,\\mu;W)$ satisfying <a href=\"#eq:compact-coboundaries\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:compact-coboundaries\">[eq:compact-coboundaries]</a> with $l=A\\pi$. There is a finite nonzero $U$-invariant measure $\\nu$ on $Y$ and functions $\\theta_j\\in L^2(Y,\\nu;W)$ such that, for every compact $Q\\subset G$, $$\\label{eq:inverse-coboundaries}\n \\sup_{y\\in Q}\n \\bigl\\|\\pi\\bar F(y)+\\theta_j\\circ U_y-\\theta_j-A^{-1}\\pi y\n \\bigr\\|_{L^2(\\nu)}\\longrightarrow0.$$ In particular, for every fixed $0<R<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:inverse-stationary-sublinear}\n \\lim_{r\\to\\infty}\\frac1r\\sup_{|y|_G\\le Rr}\n \\|\\pi\\bar F(y)-A^{-1}\\pi y\\|_{L^2(\\nu)}=0.$$",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix an invariant probability $\\mu$ on $X$, without assuming ergodicity.         "exact_quote": "Use the data of Definition <a href=\"#def:chart-profiles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:chart-profiles\">28</a>.         "exact_quote": "Let $f:\\mathcal U_\\delta\\to W$ be continuous and locally Lipschitz along horizontal segments, with locally uniform bounds for unit horizontal velocities.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-neighbor-labels",
        "exact_quote": "There is $\\delta_1>0$, depending only on the fixed group and the uniform quasi-isometry bounds, with the following property. Fix $0<\\delta\\leq\\delta_1$. Let $F$, $x$, and the following suffixes and steps vary with $r$: $$\\begin{gathered}\n a\\sim\\frac{1_{B_r(\\mathcal C)}}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}B_r(\\mathcal C)}\\,dn,\n \\qquad n=ak_0,\\qquad\n k_0\\in B_r((1+\\delta)\\mathcal C,e^{o(r)}),\\\\\n k=\\exp(tX_i),\n \\quad |t|\\leq e^{(1+\\delta)r s_i(\\mathcal C)+o(r)}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ The suffix and step are deterministic. Every pair in the support of a limiting joint law of $(f_{r,x,n},f_{r,x,nk})$ has the form $$(A\\pi z+b_1,\\ A\\pi z+b_2)\\qquad(z\\in\\mathcal U_\\delta)$$ for a common $A\\in\\mathcal A$ and some $b_1,b_2\\in W$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-neighbor-labels",
          "exact_quote": "There is $\\delta_1>0$, depending only on the fixed group and the uniform quasi-isometry bounds, with the following property. Fix $0<\\delta\\leq\\delta_1$. Let $F$, $x$, and the following suffixes and steps vary with $r$: $$\\begin{gathered}\n a\\sim\\frac{1_{B_r(\\mathcal C)}}{\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}B_r(\\mathcal C)}\\,dn,\n \\qquad n=ak_0,\\qquad\n k_0\\in B_r((1+\\delta)\\mathcal C,e^{o(r)}),\\\\\n k=\\exp(tX_i),\n \\quad |t|\\leq e^{(1+\\delta)r s_i(\\mathcal C)+o(r)}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ The suffix and step are deterministic. Every pair in the support of a limiting joint law of $(f_{r,x,n},f_{r,x,nk})$ has the form $$(A\\pi z+b_1,\\ A\\pi z+b_2)\\qquad(z\\in\\mathcal U_\\delta)$$ for a common $A\\in\\mathcal A$ and some $b_1,b_2\\in W$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-tuple-lift-volume",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a positive integer $h$ and compact sets $E_1,\\ldots,E_h\\subset W$ containing $0$. Suppose that, for each $r$, there are $M_r$ tuples $(y_1,\\ldots,y_h)\\in G^h$, separated by a sufficiently large fixed constant in the maximum product metric. Let $y_0$ be a common basepoint. Suppose each tuple admits lifts relative to $y_0$ of the form $$y_0^{-1}y_j=q(U_1\\cdots U_j,d_j),\\qquad\n U_j\\in B_r(E_j,e^{o(r)}),\\qquad\n |d_j|_D\\leq Cr,\\quad \\|\\pi d_j\\|=o(r),$$ with common bounds for all tuples. Then there is a measurable set $\\mathcal T_r\\subset N^h$ of increments satisfying the same box bounds, with possibly larger subexponential factors, such that $$\\label{eq:tuple-lift-volume}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_{N^h}(\\mathcal T_r)\\geq M_r e^{-o(r)}.$$ Every product $u_1\\cdots u_h$ from $\\mathcal T_r$ has an accompanying $d\\in D$ with $|d|_D=O(r)$ and $\\pi d=o(r)$ whose projection lies a bounded distance from one of the listed terminal points $y_0^{-1}y_h$. Moreover, the $N$-coordinate volume of all such pairs projecting into any one ball of fixed radius in $G$ is at most $e^{o(r)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cone-quotients",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S\\subset W^*$ be either the closed positive ray generated by a generator weight $\\alpha$, or the closed cone generated by two linearly independent generator weights $\\alpha,\\lambda$. The span $\\mathfrak i_S$ of the mixed words is a $\\mathfrak d$-invariant ideal of $\\mathfrak n$ containing $\\mathfrak d\\cap\\mathfrak n$. Put $$I_S=\\exp_N(\\mathfrak i_S),\\qquad L_S=N/I_S.$$ The group $I_S$ is closed, and $L_S$ is connected, simply connected, and nilpotent. Every compact subset of $L_S$ has compact lifts in $N$ and hence in $G$. There is a well-defined action of $G$ on $L_S$ under which $N$ acts by left translations and $D$ acts by its induced conjugation automorphisms. Write $$p_S(g)=g\\cdot 1_{L_S}.$$ Then $p_S(gh)=g\\cdot p_S(h)$ and the stabilizer of $1_{L_S}$ is $I_SD$. There is a linear form $\\ell_S\\in W^*$ such that, for every Haar-measurable $E\\subset L_S$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_{L_S}(g\\cdot E)\n   =e^{\\ell_S(\\pi g)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_{L_S}(E).\n \\label{eq:cone-haar-multiplier}$$ The form $\\ell_S$ is the sum of the weights of $\\mathfrak n/\\mathfrak i_S$, counted with their dimensions. It can be expressed as a nonnegative integral combination of the inside generator weights, with a positive coefficient for every inside generator. In particular, it is a positive multiple of $\\alpha$ in the ray case, and it has the form $c\\alpha+d\\lambda$, with $c,d>0$, in the two-generator case.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cone-quotients",

Every compact subset of $L_S$ has compact lifts in $N$ and hence in $G$. There is a well-defined action of $G$ on $L_S$ under which $N$ acts by left translations and $D$ acts by its induced conjugation automorphisms. Write $$p_S(g)=g\\cdot 1_{L_S}.$$ Then $p_S(gh)=g\\cdot p_S(h)$ and the stabilizer of $1_{L_S}$ is $I_SD$. It can be expressed as a nonnegative integral combination of the inside generator weights, with a positive coefficient for every inside generator. In particular, it is a positive multiple of $\\alpha$ in the ray case, and it has the form $c\\alpha+d\\lambda$, with $c,d>0$, in the two-generator case.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-strict-width-tiling",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the cone, radii, and a letter $X_i$ with weight $\\alpha\\in S$. Fix also $M>0$. For all sufficiently small fixed $\\delta>0$, the following claims hold along every sequence $r\\to\\infty$. Suppose $$k_q=\\exp(t_qX_i),\\qquad |t_q|\\le e^{M\\delta r+o(r)}.$$ There is a measurable partition of $B^I_r(T_1)$ into sets indexed by anchors $i_\\kappa\\in B^I_r(T_1)$, with $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\text{tile}_\\kappa&\\subset i_\\kappa k_q B^I_r(T_2,O(1))k_q^{-1},\n &\\#\\{\\kappa\\}&\\le e^{r(P_1-P_2)+o(r)},\n \\label{eq:cone-tiles}\\\\\n k_q^{-1}i_\\kappa k_q&\\in B^I_r(T_+,O(1)).\n \\label{eq:cone-anchor}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Moreover, if $u\\in B_r(T_2,e^{o(r)})$ and $e_r\\in N$ satisfies $\\|\\log_N e_r\\|\\le e^{M\\delta r+o(r)}$, the ideal coordinate of $ue_r$ in <a href=\"#eq:ideal-section-coordinates\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ideal-section-coordinates\">[eq:ideal-section-coordinates]</a> belongs to $B^I_r(T_2,e^{o(r)})$. These statements hold simultaneously for any fixed finite collection of the cones, templates, and their images under $\\mathcal A$, after decreasing $\\delta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-neighbor-offsets",
        "exact_quote": "There exists $\\delta_2>0$ such that, for every fixed $0<\\delta\\le\\delta_2$, the following holds. Let $r\\to\\infty$, let $F$ range over the fixed family of smoothed quasi-isometries, let $x\\in G$ vary, and let $k_0$ be any allowed deterministic suffix. For a generator letter $X_i$, put $$k=\\exp(tX_i),\\qquad |t|\\le e^{(1+\\delta)r s_i(\\mathcal C)+o(r)}.$$ Every pair in the support of any limiting joint law of $(f_{r,x,ak_0},f_{r,x,ak_0k})$ has identical affine restrictions to $\\mathcal U_\\delta$. Equivalently, on every fixed compact subset of that domain, the difference of these profiles tends to zero in probability. The conclusion also holds for suffixes and steps sampled independently of $a$, provided their bounds have a common vanishing envelope.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-neighbor-offsets",

Equivalently, on every fixed compact subset of that domain, the difference of these profiles tends to zero in probability.         "exact_quote": "Let $a$ be uniform on $B_r(\\mathcal C)$.         "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a connected simply connected nilpotent Lie group with Haar measure $\\mu$ and a left invariant Riemannian metric. Let $\\rho$ be a Radon measure on $L$. Then $$\\frac{a_j}{\\mu(E_j)}\\asymp\n \\frac{a_0}{\\mu(E_0)}$$ with constants depending only on $K$ and a large-scale volume doubling constant of $L$, independently of the shapes and diameters of the sets.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-model-reduction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a finitely generated virtually polycyclic group, and let $H$ be a finitely generated group quasi-isometric to $P$. There exist a finite-index subgroup $\\Gamma\\leq P$, a connected simply connected solvable Lie group $S$, and a connected simply connected real-triangulable unimodular Lie group $G$ such that $$\\Gamma\\text{ is a uniform lattice in }S,\n \\qquad H\\simeq_{\\mathrm{QI}}P\\simeq_{\\mathrm{QI}}S\n             \\simeq_{\\mathrm{QI}}G.$$ The group $H$ is amenable. Moreover, $\\Gamma$ has a finite classifying space and is an integral Poincaré duality group of dimension $\\dim S$.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-model-reduction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a finitely generated virtually polycyclic group, and let $H$ be a finitely generated group quasi-isometric to $P$. Moreover, $\\Gamma$ has a finite classifying space and is an integral Poincaré duality group of dimension $\\dim S$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-kernel-growth",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a connected simply connected real-triangulable unimodular Lie group with the height homomorphism $\\pi:G\\to W$, and let $f:H\\to G$ be a quasi-isometry from a finitely generated group, normalized by $f(e)=1$. Suppose $H_0\\leq H$ and $\\tau:H_0\\to W$ is a homomorphism such that, for some $D<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:recognition-corrected-height}\n \\|\\pi f(h)-\\tau(h)\\|\\leq D\\qquad(h\\in H_0).$$ Then every finitely generated subgroup of $K_0=\\ker\\tau$ has polynomial growth and is virtually nilpotent.",
        "source_id": "PM-255.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-height-kernel-growth",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be a connected simply connected real-triangulable unimodular Lie group with the height homomorphism $\\pi:G\\to W$, and let $f:H\\to G$ be a quasi-isometry from a finitely generated group, normalized by $f(e)=1$. Suppose $H_0\\leq H$ and $\\tau:H_0\\to W$ is a homomorphism such that, for some $D<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:recognition-corrected-height}\n \\|\\pi f(h)-\\tau(h)\\|\\leq D\\qquad(h\\in H_0).$$ Then every finitely generated subgroup of $K_0=\\ker\\tau$ has polynomial growth and is virtually nilpotent.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Polycyclic group** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A group is *polycyclic* if it has a finite subnormal series with cyclic factors.
- **Virtually polycyclic group** ([source](#pm-h-2)): It is *virtually polycyclic* if it contains a polycyclic subgroup of finite index.
- **Quasi-isometry** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A $(K,C)$-*quasi-isometry* $F:X\to Y$, with $K\geq1$ and $C\geq0$, satisfies $$K^{-1}d_X(x,x')-C\leq d_Y(F(x),F(x'))\leq Kd_X(x,x')+C$$ for all $x,x'\in X$, and every point of $Y$ is within distance $C$ of $F(X)$.
- **Lattice and uniform lattice** ([source](#pm-h-2)): A lattice is a discrete subgroup of finite covolume; it is uniform if the quotient is compact.
- **Real-triangulable Lie group** ([source](#pm-h-4)): We use connected simply connected *real-triangulable* Lie groups: the adjoint representation of their Lie algebra admits a simultaneously upper triangular form over $\mathbb R$.
- **Unimodularity** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A group is *unimodular* if its left Haar measure is also right invariant.
- **Canonical exponential height** ([source](#stmt-def-canonical-height)): Let $\mathfrak g$ be the Lie algebra of a real-triangulable group $G$. Denote the diagonal characters of a simultaneous real triangular form of $\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}$ by $\chi_1,\ldots,\chi_s$, including repetitions and zero characters. Put $$\mathfrak k=\bigcap_{j=1}^s\ker\chi_j, \qquad W=\mathfrak g/\mathfrak k.$$ The *exponential height map* is the homomorphism $\pi:G\to(W,+)$ whose differential is the quotient map.
- **Uniform quasi-action** ([source](#pm-h-5)): Transporting the left translation action of $H$ across a quasi-isometry gives maps of $G$ with uniform quasi-isometry bounds that respect multiplication up to a uniformly bounded error. This is a *uniform quasi-action*.
- **Cartan subalgebra** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A Cartan subalgebra of a solvable Lie algebra is a nilpotent, self-normalizing subalgebra.
- **Generalized Cartan weight space** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a real character $\gamma:\mathfrak d\to\mathbb R$, its generalized weight space in $\mathfrak g$ is $$\mathfrak g_\gamma =\{u\in\mathfrak g: (\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}(v)-\gamma(v)\mathop{\mathrm{id}})^{\dim\mathfrak g}u=0 \text{ for every }v\in\mathfrak d\}.$$
- **Intrinsic group length** ([source](#sec:models)): The notation $\|x\|_J$ denotes distance from the identity in the intrinsic metric of $J$.
- **Support exponents and accessible-volume functional** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For a nonempty compact set $E\subset W$, define $$\label{eq:support-exponents} s_i(E)=\max_{w\in E}\alpha_i(w),\qquad h_Z(E)=\sum_{X_i\text{ occurring in }Z}s_i(E),\qquad P(E)=\max_{\mathcal B}\sum_{Z\in\mathcal B}h_Z(E),$$ where occurrences are counted with multiplicity and $\mathcal B$ runs over the bases selected from $\mathcal Z$.
- **Weighted nilpotent box** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For $r\ge0$ and $L>0$, put $$\label{eq:weighted-box-definition} B_r(E,L)=\left\{\exp_N\left(\sum_{Z\in\mathcal B} t_Ze^{rh_Z(E)}Z\right): \|t_Z\|\le L\right\}, \qquad B_r(E)=B_r(E,1).$$
- **Generator product scheme** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A generator product scheme is a finite list of indices $i_1,\ldots,i_k$, with endpoint map $$\label{eq:generator-endpoint-map} (t_1,\ldots,t_k)\longmapsto \prod_{j=1}^k\exp_N(t_je^{rs_{i_j}(E)}X_{i_j}).$$
- **Inner Lebesgue volume** ([source](#pm-h-17)): For a set $A\subset\mathbb R^b$, write $$\|A\|_{\mathrm{in}} =\sup\{\|K\|:K\subset A\text{ is compact}\},$$ where $\|\cdot\|$ denotes Lebesgue measure in the indicated dimension.
- **Chart profiles** ([source](#stmt-def-chart-profiles)): Let $r\to\infty$ along a sequence. For each $r$, choose a map pair with first map $F$, a basepoint $x\in G$, and a deterministic suffix $k_0\in N$ satisfying $$\label{eq:profile-suffix} k_0\in B_r((1+\delta)\mathcal C,e^{\varepsilon_r r}), \qquad \varepsilon_r\ge0,\quad \varepsilon_r\longrightarrow0.$$ Choose $a$ uniformly with respect to Haar measure on $B_r(\mathcal C)$, and set $n=ak_0$. The chart profile is the random continuous function $$\label{eq:chart-profile} f_{r,x,n}(z)=\frac{\pi F(xn\Delta_r z)-\pi F(x)}{r}, \qquad z\in D_\infty.$$ The law of this function is taken in $C_{\mathrm{loc}}(D_\infty,W)$, with its topology of uniform convergence on compact subsets. The map, basepoint, and suffix may all vary with $r$.
- **Pure and mixed generator words** ([source](#pm-h-35)): A word in the generators $X_i$ is called *pure for $S$* if every letter has weight in $S$, and *mixed for $S$* otherwise.
- **Type F** ([source](#pm-h-50)): A group $L$ is of type $F$ if it has a finite CW classifying space.
- **Homological finiteness properties** ([source](#pm-h-50)): It is of type $\mathrm{FP}_n(\mathbb Z)$ if the trivial module $\mathbb Z$ has a projective resolution over $\mathbb ZL$ that is finitely generated through degree $n$, and of type $\mathrm{FP}_\infty(\mathbb Z)$ if this holds in every degree.
- **Integral cohomological dimension** ([source](#pm-h-50)): Its integral cohomological dimension $\operatorname{cd}_{\mathbb Z}L$ is the minimum length of a projective $\mathbb ZL$-resolution of $\mathbb Z$, or infinity if no finite-length resolution exists.
- **Integral Poincaré duality group** ([source](#pm-h-50)): We use the algebraic characterization of an integral Poincaré duality group of dimension $n$: the trivial module $\mathbb Z$ admits a finite-length resolution by finitely generated projective $\mathbb ZL$-modules, and $$H^i(L;\mathbb ZL)=0\quad(i\ne n), \qquad H^n(L;\mathbb ZL)\cong\mathbb Z\quad\text{as abelian groups}.$$
- **Elementary amenable group** ([source](#pm-h-52)): We use the term *elementary amenable* for the smallest class of groups containing finite and abelian groups and closed under subgroups, quotients, extensions, and directed unions.

For each $r$, choose a map pair with first map $F$, a basepoint $x\\in G$, and a deterministic suffix $k_0\\in N$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:profile-suffix}\n k_0\\in B_r((1+\\delta)\\mathcal C,e^{\\varepsilon_r r}),\n \\qquad \\varepsilon_r\\ge0,\\quad \\varepsilon_r\\longrightarrow0.$$ Choose $a$ uniformly with respect to Haar measure on $B_r(\\mathcal C)$, and set $n=ak_0$. The chart profile is the random continuous function $$\\label{eq:chart-profile}\n f_{r,x,n}(z)=\\frac{\\pi F(xn\\Delta_r z)-\\pi F(x)}{r},\n \\qquad z\\in D_\\infty.$$ The law of this function is taken in $C_{\\mathrm{loc}}(D_\\infty,W)$, with its topology of uniform convergence on compact subsets. The map, basepoint, and suffix may all vary with $r$.",
      "interpretation": "Random nilpotent sheets carry rescaled height functions on the fixed graded Cartan domain, with independently chosen deterministic suffixes permitted to vary along the sequence.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Chart profiles",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-35",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-35",
          "exact_quote": "A word in the generators $X_i$ is called *pure for $S$* if every letter has weight in $S$, and *mixed for $S$* otherwise.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A word in the generators $X_i$ is called *pure for $S$* if every letter has weight in $S$, and *mixed for $S$* otherwise.",
      "interpretation": "Words are separated according to whether all generator weights lie in the cone under consideration.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Pure and mixed generator words",

          "anchor": "pm-h-50",
          "exact_quote": "A group $L$ is of type $F$ if it has a finite CW classifying space.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A group $L$ is of type $F$ if it has a finite CW classifying space.",
      "interpretation": "A finite classifying space supplies the finite cohomological dimension later needed for duality transfer.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Type F",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-50",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-50",
          "exact_quote": "It is of type $\\mathrm{FP}_n(\\mathbb Z)$ if the trivial module $\\mathbb Z$ has a projective resolution over $\\mathbb ZL$ that is finitely generated through degree $n$, and of type $\\mathrm{FP}_\\infty(\\mathbb Z)$ if this holds in every degree.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "It is of type $\\mathrm{FP}_n(\\mathbb Z)$ if the trivial module $\\mathbb Z$ has a projective resolution over $\\mathbb ZL$ that is finitely generated through degree $n$, and of type $\\mathrm{FP}_\\infty(\\mathbb Z)$ if this holds in every degree.",
      "interpretation": "Finite generation of projective resolutions is the finiteness property transferred before obtaining a finite classifying space.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Homological finiteness properties",
      "scope": "paper-wide"

    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-50",

        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-50",
          "exact_quote": "Its integral cohomological dimension $\\operatorname{cd}_{\\mathbb Z}L$ is the minimum length of a projective $\\mathbb ZL$-resolution of $\\mathbb Z$, or infinity if no finite-length resolution exists.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Its integral cohomological dimension $\\operatorname{cd}_{\\mathbb Z}L$ is the minimum length of a projective $\\mathbb ZL$-resolution of $\\mathbb Z$, or infinity if no finite-length resolution exists.",
      "interpretation": "This dimension is a separate prerequisite for the duality-transfer step.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Integral cohomological dimension",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-50",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-50",
          "exact_quote": "We use the algebraic characterization of an integral Poincaré duality group of dimension $n$: the trivial module $\\mathbb Z$ admits a finite-length resolution by finitely generated projective $\\mathbb ZL$-modules, and $$H^i(L;\\mathbb ZL)=0\\quad(i\\ne n),\n \\qquad H^n(L;\\mathbb ZL)\\cong\\mathbb Z\\quad\\text{as abelian groups}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "We use the algebraic characterization of an integral Poincaré duality group of dimension $n$: the trivial module $\\mathbb Z$ admits a finite-length resolution by finitely generated projective $\\mathbb ZL$-modules, and $$H^i(L;\\mathbb ZL)=0\\quad(i\\ne n),\n \\qquad H^n(L;\\mathbb ZL)\\cong\\mathbb Z\\quad\\text{as abelian groups}.$$",
      "interpretation": "The algebraic duality convention requires a finite projective resolution and the stated group-ring cohomology, without requiring orientability.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "Integral Poincaré duality group",

      "exact_definition": "We use the term *elementary amenable* for the smallest class of groups containing finite and abelian groups and closed under subgroups, quotients, extensions, and directed unions.",

      "kind": "definition",

|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-uniform-height-error | \bigl\\|\pi F(g)-\pi F(1)-A_F\pi(g)\bigr\\| | ≤ | $B$ | The finite linear group depends on the Lie model; the error constant also depends on the quasi-isometry constants. |
| q-intrinsic-coordinate-growth | \\|v_j\\| | ≤ | $C(1+\|d\|_D)^j$ | Constants depend on the group, metric, and splitting; the bound applies throughout the filtration. |
| q-intrinsic-coordinate-reverse | \|d\|_D | ≤ | $C\left(1+\max_{1\le j\le s}\\|v_j\\|^{1/j}\right)$ | The same fixed-model coordinate constant controls the reverse length estimate. |
| q-nilpotent-ball-volume-upper | \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_D\{d:\|d\|_D\le t\} | ≤ | $C t^Q$ | The polynomial growth exponent is determined by the lower central filtration, and the estimate concerns large intrinsic radii. |
| q-nilpotent-ball-volume-lower | \mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_D\{d:\|d\|_D\le t\} | ≥ | $c t^Q$ | The lower constant is positive and depends on the fixed nilpotent metric model. |
| q-unipotent-conjugation-loss | \\|U_\gamma(d)\\|+\\|U_\gamma(d)^{-1}\\| | ≤ | $C(1+\|d\|_D)^M$ | The constants are uniform over the finite collection of weight spaces. |
| q-logarithmic-ambient-distortion | \|n\|_G | ≤ | $C\log(2+\\|\log_Nn\\|)$ | The constant is model-dependent and the bound holds for all nilpotent-factor elements. |
| q-controlled-path-separated-count | Cardinality of a separated endpoint subset | ≤ | $C(1+r)^b e^{rP(E)}$ | Path length is controlled by the scale and every relative path height lies in the scaled compact template. |
| q-bounded-height-polynomial-count | Cardinality of a separated endpoint subset at bounded height | ≤ | $C(1+t)^b$ | Constants depend on the fixed height bound, path-length factor, separation, and Lie model. |
| q-fixed-position-volume-packing | Cardinality of a separated subset at a fixed Cartan position | ≥ | $\mathop{\mathrm{vol}}_N(S)/C_a$ | The packing constant is independent of the basepoint, Cartan position, and measurable parameter set. |
| q-variable-lift-volume | Nilpotent Haar outer measure of the variable-lift set | ≤ | $C(1+T)^b e^{cH}$ | Cartan intrinsic length and height are bounded separately; the projected ball center is arbitrary. |
| q-polynomial-image-inner-volume | \|\Phi(E)\|_{\mathrm{in}} | ≥ | $C c_0^{b+1}(1+B)^{-b^2} m^{A_1}(1+R)^{-A_2}$ | Constants depend only on the fixed dimensions and degree; coefficient size, minor size, parameter volume, and containing radius remain explicit. |
| q-polynomial-image-measure-exponent | A_1 | = | $1+ad(b+1)$ | The auxiliary degree bound is the one specified in the lemma. |
| q-polynomial-image-radius-exponent | A_2 | = | $a+a^2d(b+1)+Db^2$ | The exponent uses the fixed source and target dimensions and degree bound. |
| q-backward-recurrence-mass | a_{j-1} | ≥ | $\frac{a_j^2}{2D_j}$ | The nested-set recursion is performed over the prescribed finite sequence of compactly supported increment laws. |
| q-backward-recurrence-transition | \mathbb P\{x u_j\in S_j\} | ≥ | $\tau_j$ | The lower transition bound applies at each state in the corresponding preceding nested set. |
| q-bounded-leaf-height | \|\pi F(xn)-\pi F(x)\| | ≤ | $B_N$ | The bound is uniform over maps, basepoints, and arbitrary nilpotent-factor displacements. |
| q-transferred-quasiaction-defect | \sup_{g\in G}d_G(F_hF_k(g),F_{hk}(g)) | ≤ | $D_0$ | The defect is uniform over both acting group elements and the entire Lie model. |
| q-invariant-mean-correction-error | \\|\tau(h)-s(h)\\| | ≤ | $D_1$ | The correction uses the uniform bounded additive defect and is uniform over the subgroup. |

There is a finite subgroup $\\mathcal A_G\\leq\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}(W)$ such that, for every $K\\geq1$ and $C\\geq0$, there is a constant $B=B(G,K,C)$ with the following property.           "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-canonical-height",
          "exact_quote": "Each generator weight $\\alpha_i$ vanishes on $[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d]+(\\mathfrak d\\cap\\mathfrak n)$, and inclusion of $\\mathfrak d$ into $\\mathfrak g$ induces an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:canonical-cartan-height}\n W\\cong V\\Big/\\bigcap_{i=1}^\\nu\\ker\\alpha_i,\n \\qquad V=\\mathfrak d/[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d].$$ Under this identification, $\\pi$ kills $N$ and its restriction to $D$ is the displayed quotient of its abelianization. When $\\nu=0$, the intersection on the right is all of $V$, and $W=0$; this occurs exactly when $G$ is nilpotent.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathfrak n_\\gamma=\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak g_\\gamma$ and view every weight occurring in $\\mathfrak n$ as a linear form on $W$. For fixed norms, there are constants $C,M$ such that, for every $d\\in D$ and every such weight $\\gamma$, $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-unipotent}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}\n     =e^{\\gamma(\\pi d)}U_\\gamma(d),\\qquad\n \\|U_\\gamma(d)\\|+\\|U_\\gamma(d)^{-1}\\|\n     \\le C(1+|d|_D)^M,$$ where $U_\\gamma(d)$ is unipotent. For $w\\in W$, the linear map $$\\label{eq:exact-weight-dilation}\n S_w|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}=e^{\\gamma(w)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$$ is a Lie algebra automorphism and hence a group automorphism of $N$. Both $S_w$ and conjugation by $d$ preserve Haar measure on $N$. Consequently product Haar measure $dn\\,dd$ is Haar measure on $N\\rtimes D$.*\n\n\n*The height map on $D_\\infty$ is the homomorphism induced by $V_1\\cong V\\to W$, and it satisfies $$\\label{eq:d-height-scaling}\n \\pi(\\Delta_r z)=r\\pi(z)\n \\qquad(z\\in D_\\infty, r>0).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-image",
          "exact_quote": "Fix integers $a\\ge b\\ge1$ and $D\\ge1$. There are constants $C>0$ and $A_1,A_2>0$, depending only on $a,b,D$, with the following property. Let $\\Phi:\\mathbb R^a\\to\\mathbb R^b$ be a polynomial map of degree at most $D$, all of whose coefficients have absolute value at most $B\\ge0$. Suppose that one $b$-column minor of $D\\Phi$ has a coefficient of absolute value at least $c>0$. Put $c_0=\\min\\{c,1\\}$. For every $R\\ge1$ and every Lebesgue-measurable $E\\subset[-R,R]^a$ of measure $m>0$, $$\\label{eq:polynomial-image-quantitative}\n |\\Phi(E)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\n \\ge C c_0^{b+1}(1+B)^{-b^2}\n          m^{A_1}(1+R)^{-A_2}.$$ One may take, with $d=\\max\\{1,b(D-1)\\}$, $$A_1=1+ad(b+1),\\qquad\n A_2=a+a^2d(b+1)+Db^2.$$*\n\n*In particular, let $r\\to\\infty$ along a sequence and let $\\Phi_r$ have these fixed dimensions and degree bound. Suppose their coefficients converge to those of a polynomial map which is submersive at some point. If the $E_r$ are Lebesgue-measurable and $$E_r\\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a,\\qquad\n 1\\le R_r\\le e^{o(r)},\\qquad |E_r|\\ge e^{-o(r)},$$ then $$\\label{eq:polynomial-image-subexponential}\n |\\Phi_r(E_r)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ The same conclusion follows from <a href=\"#eq:polynomial-image-quantitative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:polynomial-image-quantitative\">[eq:polynomial-image-quantitative]</a> if the coefficient upper bounds $B_r$ and a chosen minor-coefficient lower bound $c_r$ satisfy $\\log(1+B_r)+\\log(1/\\min\\{c_r,1\\})=o(r)$.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-backward-recurrence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $J$ be a second countable unimodular locally compact group with Haar measure $\\mu$. Let $Q\\subset J$ be compact with $0<\\mu(Q)<\\infty$, and let $S\\subset Q$ be Borel with $\\mu(S)>0$. Fix an integer $h\\ge1$ and compactly supported probability measures $\\sigma_1,\\ldots,\\sigma_h$ on $J$. Suppose that numbers $D_j\\ge1$ satisfy $$\\mu(Q\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma_j)\\le D_j\\mu(Q)\n       \\qquad(1\\le j\\le h).$$ There are nested Borel sets $$S_0\\subset S_1\\subset\\cdots\\subset S_h=S$$ with positive measure such that, writing $a_j=\\mu(S_j)/\\mu(Q)$ and $\\tau_j=a_j/(2D_j)$, one has $$\\label{eq:recurrence-mass}\n a_{j-1}\\ge\\frac{a_j^2}{2D_j}$$ and $$\\label{eq:recurrence-transition}\n \\mathbb P\\{x u_j\\in S_j\\}\\ge\\tau_j\n       \\qquad(x\\in S_{j-1}),$$ where $u_j$ has law $\\sigma_j*\\check\\sigma_j$. If the $u_j$ are sampled independently, every $x\\in S_0$ therefore has probability at least $\\prod_{j=1}^h\\tau_j$ of satisfying $$x u_1\\cdots u_j\\in S_j\\subset S\n       \\qquad(1\\le j\\le h).$$ In a sequence indexed by $r\\to\\infty$, if $h$ is fixed, $\\mu(S)/\\mu(Q)\\ge e^{-o(r)}$, and every $D_j\\le e^{o(r)}$, then $\\mu(S_0)/\\mu(Q)$ and the displayed success probability are at least $e^{-o(r)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "q-backward-recurrence-mass",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": null,

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cartan-model** [claim](#stmt-lem-cartan-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cartan-model): Generalized primary decomposition, Engel's theorem, and integration of nilpotent subalgebras. -> Nilpotent factors, homogeneous generators, and the surjective multiplication map with its overlap kernel.
- **step-nilpotent-scaling** [claim](#stmt-lem-d-scaling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-d-scaling): Finite Baker–Campbell–Hausdorff expansions, persistent inverse charts, and the Haar Jacobian. -> Polynomial coordinate bounds, graded scaling limits, polynomial volume, and doubling.
- **step-canonical-height** [claim](#stmt-lem-canonical-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-canonical-height): Identify adjoint characters on the Cartan factor and separate scalar and unipotent actions. -> Canonical Cartan height quotient and Haar-preserving weight actions.
- **step-box-calculus** [claim](#stmt-lem-box-calculus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-box-calculus): Maximizing-basis exchange and terminating nilpotent product expansions. -> Uniform normalized products, box volumes, and conjugation estimates.
- **step-support-symmetries** [claim](#stmt-lem-support-symmetries) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support-symmetries): Positive weight relation, polyhedral norm geometry, and support-cap comparison. -> Finite support-symmetry group preserving oriented generator rays.
- **step-generator-schemes** [claim](#stmt-lem-product-schemes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-product-schemes): Compact limits of normalized nilpotent laws and submersive conjugate-generator endpoint maps. -> Uniform product generation and fixed submersive limiting schemes.
- **step-logarithmic-distortion** [claim](#stmt-lem-log-distortion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-log-distortion): Conjugate generator factors to remove their exponential amplitudes. -> Logarithmic ambient distortion and short support-height switches.
- **step-support-paths** [claim](#stmt-lem-support-switch-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-support-switch-paths): Visit support positions along Cartan legs, switch sheets, and reverse the legs. -> Controlled paths reaching weighted boxes at a common pivot.
- **step-path-packing** [claim](#stmt-lem-path-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-path-packing): Lift bounded path increments and pack disjoint small balls in a product-Haar region. -> Upper endpoint counts and controlled relative lifts.
- **step-polynomial-path-packing** [claim](#stmt-lem-polynomial-path-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-path-packing): Bound character factors using bounded path height and retain polynomial coordinate losses. -> Polynomial separated endpoint count.
- **step-fixed-position-packing** [claim](#stmt-lem-fixed-sheet-packing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-fixed-sheet-packing): Cover a fixed-position slice by balls whose nilpotent Haar preimages have uniformly bounded volume. -> Separated-point lower bounds from actual endpoint Haar volume.
- **step-lift-multiplicity** [claim](#stmt-lem-lift-multiplicity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-lift-multiplicity): Compare lifts through bounded group differences and retain the full overlap factor. -> Projected-ball upper bounds on nilpotent lift volume.
- **step-polynomial-image** [claim](#stmt-lem-polynomial-image) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-image): Derivative-minor sublevel bounds, a positive-measure coordinate slice, and local injective change of variables. -> Quantitative and subexponential polynomial-image inner-volume bounds.
- **step-backward-recurrence** [claim](#stmt-lem-backward-recurrence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-backward-recurrence): Cauchy–Schwarz for convolution transitions followed by backward threshold selection. -> Nested starting sets and a lower successful-switch probability.
- **step-restricted-products** [claim](#stmt-cor-restricted-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-restricted-products): Convert recurrent success probability to Lebesgue measure using the density bound, then apply polynomial image volume. -> Retained successful parameter measure and endpoint inner volume.
- **step-lipschitz-pairs** [claim](#stmt-lem-lipschitz-pairs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-recognition-from-height-4): A bounded-multiplicity nerve and controlled simplex contractions smooth both coarse maps. -> Nearby uniformly Lipschitz forward and inverse maps with bounded composition errors.
- **step-pair-sections** [claim](#stmt-lem-pair-sections) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pair-sections): Arzelà–Ascoli compactness and explicit normalization coordinates. -> Compact normalized sections and continuous right actions.
- **step-cohomological-slope** [claim](#stmt-lem-cohomological-slope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cohomological-slope): Project the height cocycle to invariant vectors and use reduced-cohomology vanishing on the orthogonal complement. -> A homomorphic slope with compact coboundary approximations and averaged sublinear error.
- **step-stationary-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-stationary-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stationary-volume): Compare a positive-volume set of good endpoint images using finite path meshes and packing bounds. -> Forward support-volume inequality and factorization through invertible canonical height.
- **step-inverse-coupling** [claim](#stmt-lem-inverse-coupling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-inverse-coupling): Disintegrate the invariant Haar-product measure in inverse-section coordinates and average transfers over whole balls. -> A finite invariant inverse-section measure with precisely the inverse slope.
- **step-stationary-labels** [claim](#stmt-prop-stationary-slopes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stationary-slopes): Apply forward and coupled inverse support-volume comparisons. -> Stationary slopes lie in the finite support-symmetry group.
- **step-stationary-increments** [claim](#stmt-cor-stationary-increments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-stationary-increments): Ergodic decomposition and dominated convergence for distance to the finite increment set. -> Stationary increment convergence without an ergodicity assumption.
- **step-interior-slack** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-interior-slack) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-interior-slack): Expand suffix conjugation into finitely many bracket words and compare strict support-width gaps. -> Exponential decay in normalized chart coordinates.
- **step-occupation-invariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-occupation-invariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-occupation-invariance): Show total-variation invariance under fixed Cartan and nilpotent right moves before projection. -> Invariant weak limits of occupation laws.
- **step-profile-convex-increments** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-convex-increments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-convex-increments): Fix an occupation limit, take the geometric limit at fixed microstep length, then use stationary increment convergence. -> Convex-hull constraint on horizontal secants.
- **step-profile-segment-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-profile-segment-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-profile-segment-volume): Fix a submersive scheme before restriction, retain recurrent good sheets, and compare accessible endpoint packing. -> Lower support exponent for the profile image of each horizontal segment.
- **step-affinity-criterion** [claim](#stmt-lem-affinity-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-affinity-criterion): Finite derivative labels, equality of support and variation, extremal-direction line affinity, and hypoelliptic regularity. -> One affine height function on the chart interior.
- **step-affine-profiles** [claim](#stmt-prop-affine-profiles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-affine-profiles): Apply the deterministic affinity criterion to supported profile limits. -> Affine restrictions for every supported limiting profile.
- **step-affine-class-concentration** [claim](#stmt-cor-affine-profile-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-affine-profile-concentration): Closedness of affine restrictions and a compactness contradiction using weak convergence. -> Uniform concentration near the affine class.
- **step-tuple-lift-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-tuple-lift-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tuple-lift-volume): Thicken separated tuple lifts, integrate out polynomial-volume Cartan coordinates, and change total coordinates to increments. -> Large increment Haar volume and controlled terminal projection multiplicity.
- **step-neighbor-labels** [claim](#stmt-prop-neighbor-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-tuple-lift-volume-2): Force a facet reflection from differing labels and compare alternating source and target bundle volumes. -> Common linear labels on neighboring sheets.
- **step-cone-quotients** [claim](#stmt-lem-cone-quotients) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cone-quotients): Form the ideal generated by outside letters, quotient the nilpotent algebra, and compute the trace character. -> Nilpotent quotient action and positive Haar multiplier.
- **step-strict-width-tiling** [claim](#stmt-lem-strict-width-tiling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-strict-width-tiling): Absorb conjugation costs in strict mixed-word width gaps and use maximal disjoint translates. -> Measurable covering tiles, anchor bounds, and ideal-coordinate absorption.
- **step-neighbor-offsets** [claim](#stmt-prop-neighbor-offsets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-neighbor-offsets): Condition independent Haar probes, absorb bridge errors in ideal coordinates, and compare translated quotient Haar volumes. -> Equality of neighboring affine offsets.
- **step-suffix-concentration** [claim](#stmt-lem-suffix-concentration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-suffix-concentration): Decompose suffixes into generator steps and use a Haar density comparison to collapse profile laws to point masses. -> Suffix comparison and deterministic ordinary-chart affine approximations.
- **step-scale-stabilization** [claim](#stmt-lem-scale-stabilization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-scale-stabilization): Compare overlapping scales on spanning heights and telescope offset increments. -> A common scale threshold and uniformly sublinear offsets.
- **step-global-sublinear-height** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-sublinear) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-sublinear): Compare nearby basepoints through controlled lifts and density domination, then compare coarse-inverse compositions. -> A global label, uniform sublinear height modulus, and inverse label compatibility.
- **step-ray-boundaries** [claim](#stmt-lem-ray-boundaries) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ray-boundaries): Take contracting-ray quotient limits, prove independence of representatives, and identify inverse maps through compact shadows. -> Uniform-shadow boundary homeomorphisms.
- **step-boundary-volume** [claim](#stmt-lem-boundary-volume) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-boundary-volume): Sandwich boundary images between uniformly bounded target-action comparison sets. -> Boundary-image volume comparisons recording target trace height.
- **step-leaf-height** [claim](#stmt-lem-leaf-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-leaf-height): Use quotient-invisible generator moves, the positive relation among ray forms, and bounded product generation. -> Uniformly bounded height variation on all nilpotent fibers.
- **step-haar-averaging** [claim](#stmt-lem-haar-averaging) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-haar-averaging): Integrate translate masses over large metric balls and compare inner and outer radii using doubling. -> Shape-independent mass-to-Haar-volume comparisons.
- **step-bounded-height** [claim](#stmt-thm-bounded-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-haar-averaging-2): Pull back boundary Haar measure, average its translated masses, identify trace slopes, and extend from Cartan positions across nilpotent fibers. -> Uniform bounded canonical height error for the original quasi-isometries.
- **step-model-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-model-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-model-reduction): Use lattice realization and real-triangulable reduction, then transfer metric Følner sets and obtain manifold duality. -> Amenability, a unimodular height model, and a uniform comparison lattice with finiteness and duality.
- **step-height-kernel-growth** [claim](#stmt-lem-height-kernel-growth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-height-kernel-growth): Join subgroup word-path images by bounded geodesic segments, pack bounded-height endpoints, and apply polynomial-growth recognition. -> Intrinsic polynomial growth and virtual nilpotence of each finitely generated kernel subgroup.
- **step-elementary-amenability** [claim](#stmt-prop-recognition-from-height) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-recognition-from-height): Transport left translations, eliminate the finite linear action, and correct bounded height defects with an invariant mean. -> A finite-index height homomorphism with locally virtually nilpotent kernel and elementary amenability.
- **step-virtual-polycyclic-recognition** [claim](#stmt-thm-recognition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-recognition-from-height-2): Treat nilpotent height separately; otherwise transfer homological finiteness, obtain finite type and solvability, transfer duality, and apply solvable duality-group structure. -> A polycyclic subgroup of finite index in the comparison group.
- **step-lattice-recognition** [claim](#stmt-cor-lattice-recognition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-recognition-from-height-3): Combine solvable lattice polycyclicity and uniformity with group recognition and virtual lattice realization. -> A finite-index uniform lattice realization in a possibly different solvable Lie group.

Then $\\mathfrak n$ is a nilpotent ideal contained in $[\\mathfrak g,\\mathfrak g]$, and $\\mathfrak g=\\mathfrak n+\\mathfrak d$. There are homogeneous generators $X_1,\\ldots,X_\\nu$ of $\\mathfrak n$ whose images form a basis of $\\mathfrak n/[\\mathfrak n,\\mathfrak n]$ and whose weights $\\alpha_1,\\ldots,\\alpha_\\nu$ are all nonzero.*\n\n\n*The groups $N=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak n)$ and $D=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak d)$ are closed and simply connected, and $N$ is normal in $G$. With the conjugation action of $D$ on $N$, multiplication defines a surjective Lie homomorphism and submersion $$\\label{eq:cartan-multiplication}\n q:N\\rtimes D\\longrightarrow G,\\qquad q(n,d)=nd.$$ Its kernel is $$\\label{eq:cartan-overlap-kernel}\n \\ker q=\\{(a^{-1},a):a\\in N\\cap D\\}.$$ Moreover, $N\\cap D=\\exp_G(\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak d)$. Every compact subset of $G$ has lifts in a compact subset of $N\\rtimes D$. Finally, $N=1$ if and only if $G$ is nilpotent.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-d-scaling",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C\\ge1$, depending only on $D$, the metric, and the splitting, such that for $d=\\exp_D(\\sum_jv_j)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|v_j\\|&\\le C(1+|d|_D)^j \\quad(1\\le j\\le s),\n       \\label{eq:d-coordinate-upper}\\\\\n |d|_D&\\le C\\left(1+\\max_{1\\le j\\le s}\\|v_j\\|^{1/j}\\right).\n       \\label{eq:d-coordinate-reverse}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The group laws transported from $D$ by $\\Delta_r$ converge, with all derivatives on compact coordinate sets, to the law of $D_\\infty$ as $r\\to\\infty$. Every compact $K\\subset D_\\infty$ satisfies $\\sup_{z\\in K}|\\Delta_r z|_D=O_K(r)$ for $r\\ge1$. Conversely, for every fixed $C_0$, all $\\Delta_r^{-1}\\{d:|d|_D\\le C_0r\\}$, $r\\ge1$, lie in one compact coordinate set. If $z_r,z'_r$ belong to a fixed compact coordinate set and their coordinate difference tends to zero, then $$\\label{eq:d-scaling-equicontinuity}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}_D(\\Delta_r z_r,\\Delta_r z'_r)=o(r).$$ For $v\\in V_1$ one also has $$\\label{eq:d-horizontal-flow-limit}\n \\Delta_r^{-1}\\bigl(\\Delta_r z\\exp_D(rt v)\\bigr)\n     \\longrightarrow z\\exp_\\infty(tv),$$ uniformly when $z,t,v$ range over fixed compact sets. Haar measures on $D$ and $D_\\infty$ are Lebesgue measure in these exponential coordinates, up to fixed scalars. If both scalars are chosen equal and $Q=\\sum_jj\\dim V_j$, then $\\Delta_r$ multiplies Haar volume by $r^Q$, and $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-volume}\n c t^Q\\le \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D\\{d:|d|_D\\le t\\}\\le C t^Q \\qquad(t\\ge1)$$ for some $c>0$. In particular $D$ has doubling Haar measure: $$\\label{eq:nilpotent-doubling}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,2t)\\le C\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_D B_D(1,t)\\qquad(t>0).$$ Every conclusion of this lemma holds for an arbitrary connected, simply connected nilpotent Lie group $L$ equipped with a left invariant Riemannian metric and a splitting of its lower central filtration, with $L$ in place of $D$ and constants allowed to depend on $L$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-canonical-height",
          "exact_quote": "Each generator weight $\\alpha_i$ vanishes on $[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d]+(\\mathfrak d\\cap\\mathfrak n)$, and inclusion of $\\mathfrak d$ into $\\mathfrak g$ induces an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:canonical-cartan-height}\n W\\cong V\\Big/\\bigcap_{i=1}^\\nu\\ker\\alpha_i,\n \\qquad V=\\mathfrak d/[\\mathfrak d,\\mathfrak d].$$ Under this identification, $\\pi$ kills $N$ and its restriction to $D$ is the displayed quotient of its abelianization. When $\\nu=0$, the intersection on the right is all of $V$, and $W=0$; this occurs exactly when $G$ is nilpotent.*\n\n\n*Write $\\mathfrak n_\\gamma=\\mathfrak n\\cap\\mathfrak g_\\gamma$ and view every weight occurring in $\\mathfrak n$ as a linear form on $W$. For fixed norms, there are constants $C,M$ such that, for every $d\\in D$ and every such weight $\\gamma$, $$\\label{eq:d-polynomial-unipotent}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}\n     =e^{\\gamma(\\pi d)}U_\\gamma(d),\\qquad\n \\|U_\\gamma(d)\\|+\\|U_\\gamma(d)^{-1}\\|\n     \\le C(1+|d|_D)^M,$$ where $U_\\gamma(d)$ is unipotent. For $w\\in W$, the linear map $$\\label{eq:exact-weight-dilation}\n S_w|_{\\mathfrak n_\\gamma}=e^{\\gamma(w)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{id}}$$ is a Lie algebra automorphism and hence a group automorphism of $N$. Both $S_w$ and conjugation by $d$ preserve Haar measure on $N$. Consequently product Haar measure $dn\\,dd$ is Haar measure on $N\\rtimes D$.*\n\n\n*The height map on $D_\\infty$ is the homomorphism induced by $V_1\\cong V\\to W$, and it satisfies $$\\label{eq:d-height-scaling}\n \\pi(\\Delta_r z)=r\\pi(z)\n \\qquad(z\\in D_\\infty, r>0).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-canonical-height",
      "inputs": [
        "Generalized weights",
        "Intrinsic coordinate bounds",
        "Unimodularity"
      ],
      "method": "Identify adjoint characters on the Cartan factor and separate scalar and unipotent actions.",
      "output": "Canonical Cartan height quotient and Haar-preserving weight actions.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-canonical-height"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-box-calculus",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-canonical-height"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-box-calculus",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants depending only on the fixed Lie model with the following properties, uniformly for $r\\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$.*\n\n1.  *The unit cube in a maximizing scaled basis and the Minkowski sum of the intervals $[-e^{rh_Z(E)}Z,e^{rh_Z(E)}Z]$, $Z\\in\\mathcal Z$, contain constant multiples of one another. Different maximizing bases therefore give boxes comparable by fixed changes of $L$.*\n\n2.  *In these coordinates all Lie bracket coefficients are bounded uniformly. Multiplication and inversion in exponential coordinates are polynomials of uniformly bounded degree and coefficients. For some $C,b$, a product of $k\\ge1$ elements of $B_r(E,L)$ lies in $$B_r(E,C(1+k)^b(1+L)^b).$$*\n\n\n3.  *Writing $d_N=\\dim N$, Haar measure satisfies $$\\label{eq:box-volume}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_N B_r(E,L)\\asymp L^{d_N}e^{rP(E)}.$$*\n\n\n4.  *Exact weight dilations shift the template: $S_{rw}B_r(E,L)$ is comparable, with fixed radius factors, to $B_r(E+w,L)$. For each fixed $C_0$ there are $C,b$ such that, for $r\\ge1$ and $|d|_D\\le C_0r$, $$\\label{eq:box-conjugation}\n     \\mathop{\\mathrm{Ad}}_d B_r(E,L)\\subseteq\n     B_r\\left(E+\\frac{\\pi d}{r},C(1+r)^b(1+L)^b\\right).$$ The same assertion applies to $d^{-1}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-box-calculus",
      "inputs": [
        "Bracket words",
        "Support exponents",
        "Polynomial unipotent bounds"
      ],
      "method": "Maximizing-basis exchange and terminating nilpotent product expansions.",
      "output": "Uniform normalized products, box volumes, and conjugation estimates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-box-calculus"
    },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-symmetries",
          "exact_quote": "The function $P$ is translation invariant, depends only on the convex hull, and is Lipschitz for Hausdorff distance. The function $$p(w)=P([0,w])$$ is a polyhedral norm on $W$. The group $$\\label{eq:support-symmetry-group}\n \\mathcal A=\\{A\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{GL}}(W):P(AE)=P(E)\\text{ for every nonempty\n compact convex }E\\subset W\\}$$ is finite. Its pullback action permutes the oriented rays of the generator weights $\\alpha_i$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-product-schemes",
          "exact_quote": "For each fixed $L<\\infty$ there are integers and constants depending only on $L$ and the Lie model such that every element of $B_r(E,L)$, for every $r\\ge0$ and nonempty compact $E$, is the endpoint of a scheme of at most that many factors, with all $|t_j|$ bounded by that constant. Choices may be made Borel measurably in the endpoint.*\n\n*For any sequence of these normalized coordinate systems there is a subsequence and one fixed generator product scheme whose normalized polynomial maps converge, with bounded degree and coefficients, to a map submersive at an interior point of a fixed parameter cube. The parameter point may be chosen to map to the identity.*\n\n*If $r\\to\\infty$ and the radius is $e^{o(r)}$, generation still uses a bounded number of factors, now with amplitudes $e^{rs_i(E)+o(r)}$. Finally, every element of $N$ is a product of at most a fixed number of the elements $\\exp(tX_i)$, with unrestricted real parameters.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-product-schemes"
    },

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-log-distortion",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C$ such that $$|n|_G\\le C\\log(2+\\|\\log_Nn\\|)\\qquad(n\\in N).$$ For fixed constants controlling $|d|_D/r$ and the amplitude factor, if $|d|_D=O(r)$, $|t|\\le e^{rs_i(E)+o(r)}$, and $\\alpha_i(\\pi d)=rs_i(E)+o(r)$, then the distance between $xnd$ and $xn\\exp(tX_i)d$ is $o(r)$, uniformly in $x,n$.       "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-support-switch-paths",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-support-switch-paths",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a compact convex $E_V\\subset V$, a point $v_0\\in E_V$, a constant $L$, and pivots $d_0\\in D$ satisfying $a_D(d_0)=rv_0$ and $|d_0|_D\\le C_0r$, where $r\\ge1$. Every $u\\in B_r(\\pi E_V,L)$ can be reached from $d_0$ at endpoint $ud_0$ by a path with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *it uses a bounded number of $D$-legs and generator switches, has length at most $Cr$, and all its $D$-positions have intrinsic length at most $Cr$;*\n\n2.  *on each $D$-leg the abelianized position lies in $rE_V$; each connecting switch has length at most $C\\log(2+r)$;*\n\n3.  *for any fixed continuous homomorphism $l:G\\to W$, all path values of $l$ lie within $O_l(\\log(2+r))$ of $r\\,l(E_V)$, where $l$ on $V$ denotes its induced linear map.*\n\n*Choices may be made measurably in $u$. The analogous statements with switch length $o(r)$ hold for a subexponential box radius. In particular, for a fixed compact convex $E\\subset W$ and any pivot of intrinsic length at most $C_0r$ with $\\pi d_0\\in rE$, the same construction reaches $ud_0$, $u\\in B_r(E,L)$, with absolute heights in $rE+O(\\log(2+r))$ and total length $O(r)$. The constants in this specialization are independent of the pivot’s other abelianized coordinates. One may also use a prescribed $D$-curve of length $O(r)$ and intrinsic positions $O(r)$ whose height image attains all the required supports, by visiting and reversing portions of that curve.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-packing",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $C_0>0$ and a separation distance $a>0$. There are constants $C,b$ such that the following holds for $r\\ge1$. Let $E\\subset W$ be compact and contain zero. The endpoints of paths starting at one point $x$, of length at most $C_0r$, and satisfying $$\\pi(x^{-1}\\gamma(t))\\in rE\n \\quad\\hbox{at every point of each path}$$ have $a$-separated subsets of cardinality at most $$\\label{eq:finite-path-packing}\n C(1+r)^b e^{rP(E)}.$$ Each endpoint has a relative lift $(u,d)$ with $$u\\in B_r(E,C(1+r)^b),\\qquad |d|_D\\le Cr.$$ These containments remain valid, after increasing $C,b$, under fixed bounded right thickening in $N\\rtimes D$.*\n\n*In particular, along a sequence with relative path heights in $r(E+o(1))$ and length $O(r)$, the upper separated count is $e^{rP(E)+o(r)}$, and the lifts have $u\\in B_r(E,e^{o(r)})$, $|d|_D=O(r)$. Without a height restriction, a path of length $t$ has a relative lift with $|d|_D\\le C(1+t)$ and $\\|\\log_Nu\\|\\le e^{C(1+t)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

There are $C,b,c$ such that, for $T,H\\ge0$ and any metric ball $B_G(y,R)$, the set $$\\{u\\in N:\\text{ for some }d\\in D, |d|_D\\le T,\n \\ \\|\\pi d\\|\\le H,\\ q(u,d)\\in B_G(y,R)\\}$$ has $N$-Haar outer measure at most $C(1+T)^b e^{cH}$.     {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-image",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-polynomial-image",
          "exact_quote": "Fix integers $a\\ge b\\ge1$ and $D\\ge1$. There are constants $C>0$ and $A_1,A_2>0$, depending only on $a,b,D$, with the following property. Let $\\Phi:\\mathbb R^a\\to\\mathbb R^b$ be a polynomial map of degree at most $D$, all of whose coefficients have absolute value at most $B\\ge0$. Suppose that one $b$-column minor of $D\\Phi$ has a coefficient of absolute value at least $c>0$. Put $c_0=\\min\\{c,1\\}$. For every $R\\ge1$ and every Lebesgue-measurable $E\\subset[-R,R]^a$ of measure $m>0$, $$\\label{eq:polynomial-image-quantitative}\n |\\Phi(E)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\n \\ge C c_0^{b+1}(1+B)^{-b^2}\n          m^{A_1}(1+R)^{-A_2}.$$ One may take, with $d=\\max\\{1,b(D-1)\\}$, $$A_1=1+ad(b+1),\\qquad\n A_2=a+a^2d(b+1)+Db^2.$$*\n\n*In particular, let $r\\to\\infty$ along a sequence and let $\\Phi_r$ have these fixed dimensions and degree bound. Suppose their coefficients converge to those of a polynomial map which is submersive at some point. If the $E_r$ are Lebesgue-measurable and $$E_r\\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a,\\qquad\n 1\\le R_r\\le e^{o(r)},\\qquad |E_r|\\ge e^{-o(r)},$$ then $$\\label{eq:polynomial-image-subexponential}\n |\\Phi_r(E_r)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ The same conclusion follows from <a href=\"#eq:polynomial-image-quantitative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:polynomial-image-quantitative\">[eq:polynomial-image-quantitative]</a> if the coefficient upper bounds $B_r$ and a chosen minor-coefficient lower bound $c_r$ satisfy $\\log(1+B_r)+\\log(1/\\min\\{c_r,1\\})=o(r)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

      "output": "Quantitative and subexponential polynomial-image inner-volume bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-polynomial-image"

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-backward-recurrence",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-backward-recurrence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $J$ be a second countable unimodular locally compact group with Haar measure $\\mu$. Let $Q\\subset J$ be compact with $0<\\mu(Q)<\\infty$, and let $S\\subset Q$ be Borel with $\\mu(S)>0$. Fix an integer $h\\ge1$ and compactly supported probability measures $\\sigma_1,\\ldots,\\sigma_h$ on $J$. Suppose that numbers $D_j\\ge1$ satisfy $$\\mu(Q\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma_j)\\le D_j\\mu(Q)\n       \\qquad(1\\le j\\le h).$$ There are nested Borel sets $$S_0\\subset S_1\\subset\\cdots\\subset S_h=S$$ with positive measure such that, writing $a_j=\\mu(S_j)/\\mu(Q)$ and $\\tau_j=a_j/(2D_j)$, one has $$\\label{eq:recurrence-mass}\n a_{j-1}\\ge\\frac{a_j^2}{2D_j}$$ and $$\\label{eq:recurrence-transition}\n \\mathbb P\\{x u_j\\in S_j\\}\\ge\\tau_j\n       \\qquad(x\\in S_{j-1}),$$ where $u_j$ has law $\\sigma_j*\\check\\sigma_j$. If the $u_j$ are sampled independently, every $x\\in S_0$ therefore has probability at least $\\prod_{j=1}^h\\tau_j$ of satisfying $$x u_1\\cdots u_j\\in S_j\\subset S\n       \\qquad(1\\le j\\le h).$$ In a sequence indexed by $r\\to\\infty$, if $h$ is fixed, $\\mu(S)/\\mu(Q)\\ge e^{-o(r)}$, and every $D_j\\le e^{o(r)}$, then $\\mu(S_0)/\\mu(Q)$ and the displayed success probability are at least $e^{-o(r)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-backward-recurrence",
      "inputs": [
        "Positive-measure Borel set",
        "Compact support-expansion bounds",
        "Unimodular Haar measure"
      ],
      "method": "Cauchy–Schwarz for convolution transitions followed by backward threshold selection.",
      "output": "Nested starting sets and a lower successful-switch probability.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-backward-recurrence"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cor-restricted-products",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-restricted-products",
          "exact_quote": "Let $r\\to\\infty$ along a sequence. Fix $h\\ge1$, positive integers $a_1,\\ldots,a_h$, and $a=\\sum_j a_j$. For each $r$, let $J_r,Q_r,S_r,\\mu_r$, and $\\sigma_{j,r}$ satisfy the hypotheses of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:backward-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:backward-recurrence\">18</a>, with $$\\frac{\\mu_r(S_r)}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{\\mu_r(Q_r\\mathop{\\mathrm{supp}}\\sigma_{j,r})}{\\mu_r(Q_r)}\n       \\le e^{o(r)}.$$ For each $j$, let $\\theta_j\\in\\mathbb R^{a_j}$ have a probability law $\\nu_{j,r}$, and let the $\\theta_j$ be independent. Suppose their joint law $\\nu_r$ gives full measure to a Borel admissible domain $\\Theta_r\\subset[-R_r,R_r]^a$, is absolutely continuous with respect to Lebesgue measure, and satisfies $$1\\le R_r\\le e^{o(r)},\\qquad\n \\frac{d\\nu_r}{d\\theta}\\le e^{o(r)}\n       \\quad\\text{almost everywhere}.$$ Let $v_{j,r}:\\mathbb R^{a_j}\\to J_r$ be Borel maps whose images of the laws $\\nu_{j,r}$ are $\\sigma_{j,r}*\\check\\sigma_{j,r}$. Finally, let $\\Phi_r:\\mathbb R^a\\to\\mathbb R^b$, $1\\le b\\le a$, be polynomial maps of a fixed degree bound whose coefficients converge to a map submersive somewhere.*\n\n*There is a starting point $x_r\\in S_r$ such that the successful parameter set $$E_r=\\left\\{\\theta\\in\\Theta_r:\n x_r v_{1,r}(\\theta_1)\\cdots v_{j,r}(\\theta_j)\\in S_r\n \\text{ for every }1\\le j\\le h\\right\\}$$ satisfies $$|E_r|\\ge e^{-o(r)},\\qquad\n |\\Phi_r(E_r)|_{\\mathrm{in}}\\ge e^{-o(r)}.$$ Here $\\Phi_r$ may record a product endpoint, several successive endpoints, or a tuple of bundle products; it need not record the intermediate states used in the definition of $E_r$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-lipschitz-pairs",
          "exact_quote": "For each $K\\ge1$ and $C\\ge0$, there are constants $B<\\infty$ and $\\Lambda\\ge1$, depending only on $G,K,C$, with the following property. Every $(K,C)$ self quasi-isometry of $G$ is within distance $B$ of a map $F$ belonging to a pair $(F,\\bar F)$ such that both maps are $\\Lambda$-Lipschitz, both satisfy $$d(J(g),J(h))\\ge \\Lambda^{-1}d(g,h)-\\Lambda\n \\qquad(J=F,\\bar F),$$ and $$d(\\bar F(F(g)),g)\\le\\Lambda,\\qquad\n d(F(\\bar F(g)),g)\\le\\Lambda\n \\qquad(g\\in G).$$",

          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ be an invariant ergodic probability on $X$. For each fixed $0<R<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:stationary-sublinear}\n \\lim_{r\\to\\infty}\\frac1r\\sup_{|x|_G\\le Rr}\n \\|\\pi F(x)-l(x)\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}=0.$$ These assertions concern the fixed measure $\\mu$; no rate uniform over invariant measures is asserted.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-tuple-lift-volume",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a positive integer $h$ and compact sets $E_1,\\ldots,E_h\\subset W$ containing $0$. Suppose that, for each $r$, there are $M_r$ tuples $(y_1,\\ldots,y_h)\\in G^h$, separated by a sufficiently large fixed constant in the maximum product metric. Let $y_0$ be a common basepoint. Suppose each tuple admits lifts relative to $y_0$ of the form $$y_0^{-1}y_j=q(U_1\\cdots U_j,d_j),\\qquad\n U_j\\in B_r(E_j,e^{o(r)}),\\qquad\n |d_j|_D\\leq Cr,\\quad \\|\\pi d_j\\|=o(r),$$ with common bounds for all tuples. Then there is a measurable set $\\mathcal T_r\\subset N^h$ of increments satisfying the same box bounds, with possibly larger subexponential factors, such that $$\\label{eq:tuple-lift-volume}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_{N^h}(\\mathcal T_r)\\geq M_r e^{-o(r)}.$$ Every product $u_1\\cdots u_h$ from $\\mathcal T_r$ has an accompanying $d\\in D$ with $|d|_D=O(r)$ and $\\pi d=o(r)$ whose projection lies a bounded distance from one of the listed terminal points $y_0^{-1}y_h$.       "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-cone-quotients",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-cartan-model",
        "step-canonical-height"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cone-quotients",
          "exact_quote": "Let $S\\subset W^*$ be either the closed positive ray generated by a generator weight $\\alpha$, or the closed cone generated by two linearly independent generator weights $\\alpha,\\lambda$. The span $\\mathfrak i_S$ of the mixed words is a $\\mathfrak d$-invariant ideal of $\\mathfrak n$ containing $\\mathfrak d\\cap\\mathfrak n$. Put $$I_S=\\exp_N(\\mathfrak i_S),\\qquad L_S=N/I_S.$$ The group $I_S$ is closed, and $L_S$ is connected, simply connected, and nilpotent. Every compact subset of $L_S$ has compact lifts in $N$ and hence in $G$. There is a well-defined action of $G$ on $L_S$ under which $N$ acts by left translations and $D$ acts by its induced conjugation automorphisms. Write $$p_S(g)=g\\cdot 1_{L_S}.$$ Then $p_S(gh)=g\\cdot p_S(h)$ and the stabilizer of $1_{L_S}$ is $I_SD$. There is a linear form $\\ell_S\\in W^*$ such that, for every Haar-measurable $E\\subset L_S$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_{L_S}(g\\cdot E)\n   =e^{\\ell_S(\\pi g)}\\mathop{\\mathrm{vol}}_{L_S}(E).\n \\label{eq:cone-haar-multiplier}$$ The form $\\ell_S$ is the sum of the weights of $\\mathfrak n/\\mathfrak i_S$, counted with their dimensions. It can be expressed as a nonnegative integral combination of the inside generator weights, with a positive coefficient for every inside generator. In particular, it is a positive multiple of $\\alpha$ in the ray case, and it has the form $c\\alpha+d\\lambda$, with $c,d>0$, in the two-generator case.",
          "source_id": "PM-255.1"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-cone-quotients"

          "exact_quote": "Fix the cone, radii, and a letter $X_i$ with weight $\\alpha\\in S$. Fix also $M>0$. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-virtual-polycyclic-recognition**: Quasi-isometry to a finitely generated virtually polycyclic group forces virtual polycyclicity of the finitely generated comparison group.
- **invariant-uniform-bounded-height**: Every self quasi-isometry has a height slope in a fixed finite linear group, with a bounded height error uniform for fixed quasi-isometry constants.
- **invariant-polynomial-bounded-height-packing**: Paths with uniformly bounded relative height and controlled length have only polynomially many separated endpoints.
- **invariant-profile-affinity**: Convex increment constraints together with the segment volume inequality force a limiting profile to be affine with a label in the finite support symmetry group.
- **invariant-neighbor-profile-agreement**: Neighboring sheet profiles have identical affine restrictions in every supported joint limit when the chart enlargement and generator step satisfy the stated bounds.
- **invariant-local-kernel-growth**: A homomorphism staying a bounded distance from the projected quasi-isometry height has a kernel whose finitely generated subgroups have polynomial growth and are virtually nilpotent.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-sublinear-kernel-certificate**: Use a sublinear height estimate alone to certify polynomial growth in the height kernel. -> Sublinear height errors do not supply a fixed bound on every point of the word paths needed for polynomial packing.
- **anti-use-recognition-decision-procedure**: Treat the recognition theorem as an executable decision procedure for arbitrary group presentations. -> The theorem assumes a quasi-isometry to a virtually polycyclic comparison group and supplies a structural conclusion; it does not provide a finite procedure for establishing that hypothesis.
- **anti-use-prescribed-lattice-ambient**: Conclude that the comparison group becomes a lattice in the original ambient solvable Lie group. -> The lattice conclusion permits a different ambient Lie group.
- **anti-use-success-probability-without-rank**: Infer endpoint volume from positive successful parameter probability alone. -> A parameter set can have positive measure while its polynomial image has zero volume or loses an exponential factor.
- **anti-use-measurable-selector-density**: Insert a measurable selector of generator product representations into the restricted parameter estimate. -> Measurable endpoint choices need not induce an absolutely continuous parameter law.
- **anti-use-complementary-cartan-splitting**: Assume that the Cartan multiplication map gives unique coordinates and ignore its overlap kernel. -> The multiplication map can have a nontrivial kernel, so variable lifts may hide volume in the overlap.
- **anti-use-uniform-stationary-rate**: Exchange the geometric and microstep limits using a rate uniform over invariant probabilities. -> The stationary convergence is proved for a fixed invariant probability, so varying the probability with the microstep length is unsupported.
- **anti-use-global-suffix-supremum**: Interpret the suffix estimate as a uniform finite-scale supremum over all eventually vanishing envelopes. -> The suffix conclusion is sequential and does not allow unrestricted envelope variation at a fixed scale.
- **anti-use-finitely-generated-kernel**: Promote local virtual nilpotence to virtual nilpotence of the entire height kernel or assume its image is discrete. -> The argument permits a kernel that is not finitely generated and a height image that is not discrete.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The recognition and uniform bounded-height conclusions concern infinite groups and globally quantified quasi-isometries, rather than a finite constructive input-output problem. The proof extracts stationary slopes through reduced cohomology and corrects height defects using an invariant mean: "Let $m$ be a left-invariant mean on bounded real functions on $H_0$, extended coordinatewise to functions with values in $W$." Finite polynomial coordinate constructions within the proof do not provide an executable recognition procedure or a finite oracle for the main conclusions.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Virtual-polycyclic recognition is a structural implication under an assumed quasi-isometry. The source does not supply a program that establishes this hypothesis from a group presentation. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Chart profiles describe asymptotic height functions on continuous Lie groups. Their limiting laws provide no finite simulation update rule or numerical error contract. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The replacement of coarse maps by Lipschitz representatives proves existence with uniform bounds. It does not specify a finite input representation or a procedure for computing those representatives. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Uniform product schemes provide mathematical generation and measurable parameter choices, but the source gives no effective procedure that accepts represented endpoints and returns certified parameters. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The Cartan model supplies subgroup coordinates through a surjective multiplication map with an overlap kernel. This is an algebraic coordinate model, without a specified finite storage representation or canonical encoding. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nilpotent scaling concerns convergence of group laws and polynomial Haar-volume estimates. It specifies no discrete arithmetic kernel, memory layout, or hardware execution contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The height correction uses amenability and a left-invariant mean to obtain a homomorphism. This existence argument supplies no computational hardness assumption, cryptographic primitive, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Affine-profile concentration concerns Haar-sampled charts and asymptotic probability under fixed geometric hypotheses. It provides no training procedure, learned estimator, or finite-sample prediction guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The support symmetry group preserves a geometric volume functional on compact convex height sets. The result does not define an operational optimisation problem with represented inputs and computed optimal outputs. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A transferred group action respects multiplication up to a uniform metric error. This quasi-action contract does not describe replicated state, message delivery, or convergence of distributed updates. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper directly supports geometric group theory through uniform bounded-height rigidity. Its global existence statement over self quasi-isometries is not a finite engineering construction with computable witnesses and an executable verification contract. |
